<!-- conv-1: body_md -->
\label{conv:char-zero}
For the rest of this 
paper, even if it is not explicitly noted, by ``field''
we will mean ``field of characteristic zero''.
<!-- end conv-1 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:delta-and-delta-sigma-rings}  
Let $m \in \mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$ be a natural number.  We write $\Delta = \{ \delta_1, \ldots, \delta_m\}$
for a set of $m$ symbols for derivations.  By a $\Delta$-ring we mean a commutative ring $R$ 
equipped with commuting derivations $\delta_i^R:R \to R$ for $1 \leq i \leq m$.   From now on we 
will simply write $\delta_i:R \to R$ for the derivation on $R$.  A \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ring 
is a $\Delta$-ring $(R, \delta_1, \ldots, \delta_m)$ given together with a distinguished 
ring endomorphism $\sigma:R \to R$ which commutes with all of the distinguished derivations 
$\delta_i:R \to R$.  A \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ring $R$ is
\emph{inversive} if $\sigma$ is an automorphism.  A $\Delta$-field, respectively,  (inversive) 
\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field, is a 
$\Delta$-ring, respectively, 
(inversive)  \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ring, whose underlying ring is a field.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- conv-2: body_md -->
\label{conv:inversive-difference}
Henceforth, when we say ``\(\sigma\)-ring''
or ``\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ring''
or ``\(\sigma\)-whatever'', 
we mean \emph{inversive} 
\(\sigma\)-ring, \emph{inversive}
\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ring, or 
\emph{inversive} \(\sigma\)-whatever.
That is, we require \(\sigma\) to 
be an \emph{automorphism}.
<!-- end conv-2 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
In the language of our 
introduction, we would refer to a $\Delta$-field 
as a differential field and a \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field
as a difference-differential field. 
Maintaining ``$\Delta$'' 
as part of the notation recalls that we have 
$m$ distinguished derivations.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:delta-poly}  
Given a $\Delta$-ring \(R\) and a 
tuple $x = (x_1, \ldots, x_n)$ of variables, the 
$\Delta$-ring
of $\Delta$-polynomials over $R$ is the free $\Delta$-$R$-algebra generated by $x$.  We 
denote this algebra  by $R \{ x\}_\Delta$.  As an 
$R$-algebra, $R \{ x \}_{\Delta}$ is the polynomial ring
in the formal variables $\delta_1^{\ell_1} \cdots \delta_m^{\ell_m} x_i$ for $(\ell_1, \ldots, \ell_m) \in 
\mathbb{N}^m$ and $1 \leq i \leq n$.  
From the universal mapping property of a free algebra, 
whenever $R$ is a $\Delta$-ring, $f:R \to S$ is a homomorphism of
$\Delta$-rings, and $a \in S^n$ is an $n$-tuple
from $S$, then there is a unique \(\Delta\)-ring homomorphism
$\overline{f}:R\{x\}_\Delta \to S$
extending $f$ and having $\overline{f}(x_i) = a_i$ for 
$1 \leq i \leq n$.   For $P \in R\{x\}_{\Delta}$,  
we write $P(a) := \overline{f}(P)$.  

When \(R\) is a 
\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ring, we have a similar construction of the 
ring of \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-polynomials
in the variables \(x\), \(R \{x\}_{\sigma,\Delta}\), as the free 
inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-\(R\)-algebra generated by \(x\).  As an 
\(R\)-algebra, \(R \{x\}_{\sigma,\Delta}\) is the 
polynomial ring over \(R\) in the formal variables $\sigma^{\ell_0} \delta_1^{\ell_1} \cdots \delta_m^{\ell_m} x_i$ for $(\ell_0,\ell_1, \ldots, \ell_m) \in 
\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{N}^{m}$ and $1 \leq i \leq n$.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- conv-3: body_md -->
For the sake 
of simplifying some of our statements and definitions, 
let us fix a universal domain \(\mathbb{U}\) for a chosen
completion of \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\).  That is, 
\(\mathbb{U}\) is a model of \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\)
and if \(K \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) is an algebraically closed 
``small'', say, \(|K| < |\mathbb{U}|\), \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-subfield of 
\(\mathbb{U}\) and \(K \subseteq L\) is an extension to another
small \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field then there is an embedding of 
\(L\) into \(\mathbb{U}\) over \(K\).  Forgetting \(\sigma\), 
\(\mathbb{U}\) is also a universal domain for \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\).
<!-- end conv-3 -->
<!-- nota-1: body_md -->
\label{nota:ast-for-delta}
We will have occasion to compare notions defined for \(\Delta\)-fields and
for \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-fields.  Often, these concepts are 
defined in analogous ways.  We will write a simple asterisk, ``\(\ast\)'', 
for ``\(\Delta\)'' or ``\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)''.
Thus ``\(\ast\)-field'' is 
to be read as ``\(\Delta\)-field'' or ``inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field'' as the 
context demands.
<!-- end nota-1 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
For \(A \subseteq \mathbb{U}\)
we define \(\langle A \rangle_\ast\) to be the \(\ast\)-field
generated by \(A\) in \(\mathbb{U}\).   We define \(\operatorname{acl}^\ast(A)\) to be the 
field-theoretic algebraic closure of \(\langle A \rangle_\ast\). 
For a tuple $a$ from $\mathbb{U}$ and a $\ast$-subfield $K\subseteq\mathbb{U}$ we write $K\langle a\rangle_\ast := \langle K\cup\{a\}\rangle_\ast$.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
The set  \(\operatorname{acl}^\ast(A)\) is the model-theoretic algebraic closure of \(A\) relative to
\(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\) or \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\), respectively.  That is, 
\(\operatorname{acl}^\ast(A)\) is the set of elements of \(\mathbb{U}\) which 
belong to some \emph{finite} definable set, defined using parameters
from \(A\), again relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\) or \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\), respectively.
Equivalently, \(\operatorname{acl}^\ast(A)\) is the set of elements of \(\mathbb{U}\)
whose orbit under \(\operatorname{Aut}^\ast(\mathbb{U}/A)\), the
group of automorphisms of \(\mathbb{U}\) as a \(\ast\)-field
fixing \(A\) pointwise, is finite.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
Let us note that $\langle A \rangle_\Delta=\mathbb{Q}\left(\delta_1^{\ell_1} \cdots \delta_m^{\ell_m}(a): a\in A, \ell_i\in\mathbb N\right)$ and  \(\langle A \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\) \(= \mathbb{Q}\left(\bigcup_{n \in \mathbb{Z}} \sigma^n(\langle A \rangle_\Delta)\right)\).
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def:ast-ind}    
For \(A\), \(B\), and \(C\) small subsets of $\mathbb{U}$,
we say that \(A\) is \(\ast\)-free from \(B\) over \(C\), written \(A \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\ast_C B\), 
if the field \(\langle A \cup C\rangle_\ast\) is algebraically independent from 
\(\langle B \cup C \rangle_\ast\) over \(\langle C \rangle_\ast\).
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rk:properties-of-independence}
The independence relations \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\ast\) for \(\ast = \Delta\) or 
\(\ast=\sigma\text{-}\Delta\) agree with forking independence as 
defined in general simple theories.  See~\cite[Proposition 3.1(v)]{LS-DCFAm}.
Since \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\ast\) agrees with forking independence in a simple theory, it satisfies some useful properties.
For example, it is symmetric in the sense that for any sets \(A\), \(B\), and \(C \subseteq \mathbb{U}\)
we have \[A {\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B}^\ast C \Longleftrightarrow C {\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B}^\ast A .\]  In 
our concrete case, it is easy to deduce this symmetry from the evident symmetry of algebraic independence.  
Another useful property is transitivity of independence:  if \(B \subseteq C \subseteq D \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) and 
\(A \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) then we have \[A {\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B}^\ast D \Longleftrightarrow (A {\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B}^\ast C \text{ and } A {\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_C}^\ast D) .\]
As with symmetry, transitivity for \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\ast\) is inherited from transitivity for algebraic 
independence.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rk:general-stable}  
With the exception of the analysis of specific differential equations carried out in 
Section~\ref{sec:examples-of-differential-equations}, almost everything
we do makes sense for expansions of models of general stable theories by automorphisms.   That is, 
if we allow \(\mathbb{U}\) to be a large saturated model with a stable theory and \(\sigma:\mathbb{U} \to 
\mathbb{U}\) to be a generic automorphism of \(\mathbb{U}\), then we may define an independence
relation \(A \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B^\sigma C\) on triples of small subsets of \(\mathbb{U}\) by the rule that 
\[A {\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B}^\sigma C \Longleftrightarrow \operatorname{acl}(\bigcup_{n \in \mathbb{Z}} \sigma^n(A \cup B)) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_{\operatorname{acl}(\bigcup_{n \in \mathbb{Z}} \sigma^n(B))}  \operatorname{acl}(\bigcup_{n \in \mathbb{Z}} \sigma^n(B \cup C)) \]
where here \(\operatorname{acl}\) is the model theoretic algebraic closure in \(\mathbb{U}\) and 
\(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}\) is forking independence relative to the theory of \(\mathbb{U}\).  As in Remark~\ref{rk:properties-of-independence}
it is easy to check that \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\sigma\) satisfies symmetry and transitivity.  Since our principal motivation is to understand 
differential equations, our results are written for \(\Delta\)-fields and \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-fields, but the interested reader 
can easily generalize to arbitrary expansions of stable structures by automorphisms by replacing 
\(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta\) with \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}\) and \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\) by \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\sigma\).
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{def:ideal}
Let $K$ be a $\ast$-field and let $x = (x_1, \ldots, x_n)$
be a tuple of variables.
For a point $a \in \mathbb{U}^n$, we 
let $I_\ast(a/K)$ be the \(\ast\)-ideal in 
\(\mathbb{U} \{ x \}_\ast\) generated by 
\(\{ P \in K \{ x \}_\ast : P(a) = 0 \}\).
For  $S \subseteq \mathbb{U} \{ x \}_\ast$ a set of
$\ast$-polynomials we let  
$\mathbb V(S)(\mathbb{U}) := \{ b \in \mathbb{U}^n : P(b) = 0 
\text{ for all } P \in S \}$.  Usually, we will write 
$X =\mathbb V (S)$ and then $X(K)$ for $\mathbb V(S)(K) := \mathbb V(S)(\mathbb{U}) \cap K^n$.  
We will say 
that $X$ is defined by $S$ and that \(X\) is defined over \(K\) if
\(S \subseteq K \{ x \}_\ast\).
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- conv-4: body_md -->
From now on, we use the term ``definable set'' to mean 
``quantifier-free definable set relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\) or \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\)''
as the context demands.
<!-- end conv-4 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
A \emph{(quantifier-free) partial $\ast$-type} in the
variables $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$ is a small,  
consistent set $\Sigma$ of equations
$\ulcorner P(x)=0\urcorner$ and inequations
$\ulcorner P(x)\neq 0\urcorner$, where
$P\in\mathbb U\{x\}_\ast$ is a $\ast$-polynomial.
Here, consistency means that for any finite subset \(\Sigma_0 \subseteq \Sigma\) 
there is a solution \(a \in \mathbb{U}^n\)
to all the equations \(P(a) = 0\) and inequations \(Q(a) \neq 0\) with 
\(\ulcorner P(x) = 0 \urcorner \in \Sigma_0\) and 
\(\ulcorner Q(x) \neq 0 \urcorner \in \Sigma_0\).
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
If \(\Sigma\) is a partial type, then 
there is a solution to all of \(\Sigma\) in 
\(\mathbb{U}\) by our universality hypothesis on 
\(\mathbb{U}\).  We refer to such a 
solution as a \emph{realization} of \(\Sigma\).  We 
write \(\Sigma(\mathbb{U})\) for the set of 
realizations of \(\Sigma\) in \(\mathbb{U}\).
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
Let $K\subseteq\mathbb U$ be a small $\ast$-subfield.
A partial $\ast$-type $\Sigma$ is \emph{over $K$} if 
\(P \in K \{x\}_\ast\) for every 
equation \(\ulcorner P(x) = 0 \urcorner \)
and inequation \(\ulcorner P(x) \neq 0 \urcorner \)
in \(\Sigma\).
We say that $\Sigma$ is \emph{complete} if, for every
$P\in K\{x\}_\ast$, exactly one of
$\ulcorner P(x)=0\urcorner$ and
$\ulcorner P(x)\neq 0\urcorner$ belongs to $\Sigma$.
We write $S_{x,\ast}(K)$ for the space of complete quantifier-free
$\ast$-types over $K$ in the variables $x$, or simply $S_{\ast}(K)$ when the tuple $x$ is clear.
                                                          

For $a\in\mathbb U^n$, we define its $\ast$-type over $K$ by
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{tp}_\ast(a/K)
&:= \bigl\{
  \ulcorner P(x)=0\urcorner :
  P\in K\{x\}_\ast,\ P(a)=0
  \bigr\}\\
&\quad\cup \bigl\{
  \ulcorner P(x)\neq 0\urcorner :
  P\in K\{x\}_\ast,\ P(a)\neq 0
  \bigr\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
If the partial type \(\Sigma\) is finite, then 
the set of realizations \(\Sigma(\mathbb{U})\) is 
a definable set.  In general, for any partial type
\(\Sigma\), we have that \(\Sigma(\mathbb{U}) = \bigcap_{\Sigma_0 \subseteq \Sigma, \text{ finite }} \Sigma_0(\mathbb{U})\)
expresses the set of realizations of \(\Sigma\) as an intersection of definable sets.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
It follows from the saturation hypothesis on 
\(\mathbb{U}\) that if \(K \subseteq \mathbb{U}\)
is a small \(\ast\)-subfield then every type in 
\(S_{x,\ast}(K)\) is of the form \(\operatorname{tp}_\ast(a/K)\)
for some \(a \in \mathbb{U}^n\).
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
If $K$ is a small $\ast$-field, there is a natural map
from $S_{x,\ast}(K)$ to the set
$\operatorname{Spec}^{\ast}(K\{x\}_{\ast})$
of prime $\ast$-ideals, given by
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathfrak{p}\colon S_{x,\ast}(K)
&\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Spec}^{\ast}(K\{x\}_{\ast}),\\
p
&\longmapsto
\bigl\{
P\in K\{x\}_{\ast}
:
\ulcorner P(x)=0\urcorner\in p
\bigr\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
If \(\ast = \Delta\), then \(\mathfrak{p}\) is 
a bijection.  In general, if \(K\) is algebraically
closed, \(\mathfrak{p}\) is still a bijection,
though when \(\ast = \sigma\text{-}\Delta\) and 
\(K\) is not algebraically closed, then because there 
may be extensions of \(\sigma\) to the 
algebraic closure of \(K\) which are not conjugate 
to the specified extension computed from the 
algebraic closure of \(K\) inside of \(\mathbb{U}\), 
some prime \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-ideals
in \(K\{x\}_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\) might 
not be in the image of \(\mathfrak{p}\).

If the type \(p \in S_{x,\ast}(K)\) is realized by 
\(a \in \mathbb{U}^n\), so that \(p = \operatorname{tp}_\ast(a/K)\), 
then \(I_\ast(a/K)\) is the ideal generated by \(\mathfrak{p}(p)\) in 
\(\mathbb{U}\{x\}_\ast\).
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop-nonforking-ideal}
Let \(K \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) 
be an algebraically closed 
\(\ast\)-field, \(n \in \mathbb{N}\), 
\(a \in \mathbb{U}^n\), and 
\(K \subseteq L \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) an 
extension of \(\ast\)-fields.  Then 
\(a \setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0^\ast_K L\) if and only if 
\(I_\ast(a/K) = I_\ast(a/L)\).
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Set $F:=K\langle a\rangle_\ast$, $J_K:=\{P\in K\{x\}_\ast:P(a)=0\}$ and
$J_L:=\{P\in L\{x\}_\ast:P(a)=0\}.$ 
Since $K$ is algebraically closed, $a\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0^\ast_K L$
is equivalent to linear disjointness of
$F$ and $L$ over $K$.   We show that this is equivalent to 
\(J_L=J_K\cdot L\{x\}_\ast\), i.e., \(I_\ast(a/K) = I_\ast(a/L)\) after extending to $\mathbb U\{x\}_\ast$.  Suppose first that $F$ and $L$ are linearly disjoint
over $K$. For $P\in J_L$, choose a $K$-basis
$\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r$ of the span of its
coefficients and write
\(
P=\sum_{i=1}^r\lambda_iP_i, P_i\in K\{x\}_\ast.
\)
By linear disjointness, the $\lambda_i$ remain
linearly independent over $F$.
Thus $P_i(a)=0$ for every $i$, which proves \(J_L=J_K\cdot L\{x\}_\ast\). Conversely, $J_L=J_KL\{x\}_\ast$ implies that a
$K$-basis of $K\{a\}_\ast$ remains linearly independent
over $L$, so, by clearing denominators, $F$ and $L$
are linearly disjoint over $K$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
Let \(K\) be a small \(\ast\)-field and \(p \in S_{x,\ast}(K)\) a
complete \(\ast\)-type over \(K\).  We say that \(p\) is
\emph{algebraic} if every component of a realization of \(p\) 
belongs to \(K^{\operatorname{alg}}\).  We say that \(p\) is 
\emph{minimal} if for every \(\ast\)-field \(L\) with \(K \subseteq L \subseteq \mathbb{U}\)
there is a unique non-algebraic type \(q \in S_{x,\ast}(L)\) with \(p \subseteq q\).
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{remark:alg-minimality}
For \(K\) an algebraically closed 
\(\ast\)-subfield of \(\mathbb{U}\), the type \(p \in S_{x,\ast}(K)\) is minimal if and only if it has infinitely many realizations in 
\(\mathbb{U}\) but for any \(P \in \mathbb{U} \{x\}_\ast \backslash (\mathfrak{p}(p)\cdot \mathbb{U} \{x\}_\ast)\), \(P(x) = 0\) has at most finitely many solutions 
in \(p(\mathbb{U})\).  In particular, if the transcendence degree of \(\operatorname{Frac}(K \{x\}_\Delta/\mathfrak{p}(p))\) 
 over \(K\) is one, then \(p\) is a minimal type. The converse fails as, for instance, the generic type of the third order algebraic differential equation satisfied by the $j$-function is minimal~\cite{FS2018}, although \(
 \operatorname{tr.deg}_K
\operatorname{Frac}\bigl(K\{x\}_\Delta/\mathfrak{p}(p)\bigr)=3.
\)
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
Let \(K\) be a small \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-subfield of \(\mathbb{U}\) and \(p \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)\) and \(q \in S_{y,\Delta}(K)\) two
minimal types over \(K\) in the variables \(x = (x_1, \ldots, x_n)\) and \(y = (y_1, \ldots, y_\ell)\), respectively.
We say that \(p\) and \(q\) are \emph{weakly orthogonal}, written \(p \perp^{\operatorname{w}} q\), if whenever \(a \in \mathbb{U}^n\) and \(b \in \mathbb{U}^\ell\) realize \(p\) and \(q\), respectively, then \(a \setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0^\Delta_K b\).    We say that \(p\) and \(q\) are 
\emph{orthogonal}, written \(p \perp q\), if for any small \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field  extension \(L\) of $K$ and any non-forking extensions
\(\widetilde{p} \in S_{x,\Delta}(L)\) and \(\widetilde{q} \in S_{y,\Delta}(L)\) of \(p\) and \(q\) we have 
\(\widetilde{p} \perp^{\operatorname{w}} \widetilde{q}\).
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{rk:alg-non-orthogonal}
We may understand (non)-orthogonality algebraically.  If \(K\) is an algebraically closed 
\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field and \(p \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)\) and \(q \in S_{y,\Delta}(K)\) 
are two minimal types, then \(p \not \perp^{\operatorname{w}} q\) if and only if there 
are realizations \(a\) and \(b\) of \(p\) and \(q\) so that \(\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{a\}) = \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{b\})\).
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{example:weakly-orthogonal}
Let \(K := \mathbb{Q}^{\operatorname{alg}}\) be the algebraic closure of the rational numbers regarded as a 
\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-subfield of \(\mathbb{U}\).  Let \(m = n = \ell = 1\).  Take \(p := p_{\mathsf{C},K}\) to be  the generic type of the constants.  That is, 
\(p\) is generated by the equation \(\delta_1 x_1 = 0\) and the inequations \(f(x_1) \neq 0\) for every 
nonzero polynomial \(f \in \mathbb{Q}^{\operatorname{alg}}[x_1]\).    Let \(q\) be the type isolated by 
the equation \(\delta_1(y_1) = y_1\) and the inequation \(y_1 \neq 0\).  We see that \(p\) and \(q\) are minimal types from the 
transcendence degree criterion of Remark~\ref{remark:alg-minimality}.

Then \(p \perp^{\operatorname{w}} q\) since if \(a\) is a 
realization of \(p\), then the derivation on the 
field \(\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(\mathbb{Q}^{\operatorname{alg}} \cup \{a \}) = \mathbb{Q}(a)^{\operatorname{alg}}\)
is trivial as \(a\) is a constant, but the realizations of \(q\) are nonconstant.  On the other hand, 
\(p \not \perp q\) for if we take \(L\) to be generated over \(K\) by one solution \(d\) to \(q\), let \(a\) be a
constant which is not algebraic over \(L\), and set \(b := ad\), then \(\operatorname{tp}(b/L)\) is a minimal type extending
\(q\) though visibly \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_L b\).
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
\label{def:trivial} 
Let \(K\) be a small \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-subfield of \(\mathbb{U}\) and \(p \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)\) a minimal 
type over \(K\) relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\).  We say that \(p\) is \emph{geometrically trivial}, or
\emph{disintegrated}, if every pairwise independent set of
realizations of \(p\) is independent over \(K\).
Explicitly, for any set \(S\) of realizations of \(p\), if
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K b\) for all distinct \(a,b \in S\), then
\(
a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K \bigl(S \smallsetminus \{a\}\bigr)\)
 for every   \(a \in S.\)
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
If \(K\) is any small \(\Delta\)-field and \(q \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)\) 
is a geometrically trivial minimal type, then 
\(p_{\mathsf{C},K} \perp q\).  This follows from the facts that geometric triviality is preserved under nonorthogonality of minimal types and  that \(p_{\mathsf C,K}\) is not geometrically trivial.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
\label{def:totally-disintegrated}
Let \(K\) be a small \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-subfield of \(\mathbb{U}\) and \(p \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)\) a minimal 
type over \(K\) relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\).  We say that \(p\) is \emph{totally disintegrated} if
every set of realizations of \(p\) is independent.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
In Definition~\ref{def:totally-disintegrated} we could have required merely that the type \(p\) is non-algebraic:  minimality follows
from the requirement that every set of distinct
realizations of $p$ is independent over $K$ relative
to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\cite[Lemma 1.4]{Baldwin-Harrington} \label{lemma-trivialregular}
 Let $p\in S(A)$ be trivial and regular.
 If $a$ realizes $p$ and for some $B_0, B_1$ with $B_0\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_1$, $a\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_0\cup B_1$, then $a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_0$ or $a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_1$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
\label{def:regular}
A type \(p \in S_x(A)\) is 
\emph{regular} if for any parameter 
set \(B \supseteq A\) extending 
\(A\) and pair of realizations \(a\)
and \(b\) of \(p\), if 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B\) and \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B\),
then \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B b\).
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:minimal-to-regular}
If \(K\) is a small \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field and \(p \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)\) is 
a minimal type over \(K\), then 
\(p\) is regular.
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let \(K \subseteq B \subseteq \mathbb{U}\)
and \(a\) and \(b\) be two realizations
of \(p\) where \(B\) is a small set, 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta B\) and \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta B\).  
As noted before, the independence
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta B\) means 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta B\) or from 
the definition \( \langle K \cup \{a\} \rangle_\Delta\) is algebraically 
independent from \(L := \langle B \rangle_\Delta\) over \(K\).  
Since algebraic independence is 
insensitive to replacing a field
by its algebraic closure, we 
have \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L^{\operatorname{alg}}\).
On the other hand, since \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta B\), 
we have that \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\). 
By minimality of \(p\), 
\(\operatorname{tp}(a/L)\) is the
unique nonalgebraic extension of \(p\)
to \(S_{x,\Delta}(L)\). As 
\(\operatorname{tp}(b/L)\) is a
different extension of \(p\) to 
\(S_{x,\Delta}(L)\), it must 
be algebraic.  That is, the 
components of \(b\) belong to 
\(\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(B) = L^{\operatorname{alg}}\).  Thus, 
as \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L^{\operatorname{alg}}\)
and \(B \cup \{ b \} \subseteq L^{\operatorname{alg}} \), we conclude \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta B \cup \{ b \} \), 
and hence \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B^\Delta b\) by transitivity or base monotonicity, as
required.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:trivial-field-of-definition-of-dependence}
Suppose that \(p\) and \(q\) are
trivial regular types over the set
of parameters \(A\), \(A \subseteq B\), 
\(a\) is a realization of \(p\) and 
\(b\) is a realization of \(q\), 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B\), \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B\), but
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B b\), then \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A b\).
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
By Lemma~\ref{lemma-trivialregular} applied with $B_0 = B$ and $B_1=\{b\}$, 
if \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A b\), because \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B\),
we would have \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B \cup \{b\}\).
But then by monotonicity, 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_B b\), contrary to our 
hypothesis.  Hence, 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A b\).
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:indep-trivial}
Let \(A\) be a set of parameters  
and let \(p_i \in S_{x_i}(A)\) be 
trivial, regular types over \(A\)
for \(1 \leq i \leq n\).  Suppose that
\(b_i\) is a realization of \(p_i\)
for \(1 \leq i \leq n\) and that 
\(b_i \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A b_j\) for 
\(i \neq j\).  Then \(\{b_1, \ldots, 
b_n \}\) is independent as a set.  
That is, \(b_i \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A \{b_j : j \neq i \}\) for each \(i \leq n\).
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
We prove this result by induction 
on \(n\).  The cases of \(n \leq 2\)
are trivial.  Consider the inductive 
case of \(n+1\).  If 
\( \{b_1, \ldots,b_{n+1}\}\) were
not independent, then possibly 
at the cost of reordering the types, 
we would have \(b_{n+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A \{b_1, 
\ldots, b_{n}\}\).  Take \(B_0 := 
\{ b_n \}\) and \(B_1 := \{ b_1, 
\ldots, b_{n-1}\}\). By our 
inductive hypothesis, 
\(B_0 \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_1\).  We also 
have \(b_{n+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_0\), by our
hypothesis of pairwise
independence, and \(b_{n+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_1\)
from the inductive hypothesis.  
Lemma~\ref{lemma-trivialregular}
gives that 
\(b_{n+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A B_0 \cup B_1\), 
that is, \(b_{n+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_A 
\{b_1, \ldots, b_n \}\), contrary
to our supposition.  Hence, 
\(\{b_1, \ldots, b_{n+1}\}\)
is independent and we conclude.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:dependence-totally-disintegrate}
Let \(K \subseteq \mathbb{U}\)
be a small algebraically closed \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field
and let \(p \in S_{\Delta}(K)\) and 
\(q \in S_{\Delta}(K)\) be two 
minimal, totally disintegrated
types over \(K\) relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$.  If 
\(p \not \perp q\), then there 
are \(\Delta\)-rational maps
\(f\) and \(g\) defined over \(K\) so that
for \(a\) any realization of \(p\), 
\(f(a)\) is a realization of \(q\),  
for \(b\) any realization of \(q\), 
\(g(b)\) is a realization of \(p\), and 
\(a = g(f(a))\) and \(b = f(g(b))\).
Moreover, there is at most one 
such pair of \(\Delta\)-rational 
maps on the types for \(p\) and \(q\).
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let \(L \supseteq K\) be a 
 small \(\Delta\)-field extension of \(K\),
 \(a\) a realization of \(p\), and \(b\)
 a realization of \(q\) witnessing that 
 \(p \not \perp q\).  That is, \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\), 
 \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\), but \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_L^\Delta b\).
 By Corollary~\ref{cor:trivial-field-of-definition-of-dependence}, 
 \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta b\).  Thus, by 
 Remark~\ref{rk:alg-non-orthogonal},
 we would have \(\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{a\}) = 
 \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{b\})\). Let us 
 check that, in fact, \(K \langle a \rangle_\Delta = K \langle b \rangle_\Delta\).
 If \(b \notin K \langle a \rangle_{\Delta}\), then 
 there would be some \(b' \neq b\) and
 \(\tau\in\operatorname{Gal}(K \langle a \rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}} / K \langle a \rangle_\Delta)\) such that $\tau(b)=b'$.  As the \(\Delta\)-field structure
 on any \(\Delta\)-field of characteristic
 zero extends uniquely to an algebraic 
 closure, $\tau$ is also an automorphism of 
 \(\Delta\)-fields.  Hence, \(b'\) is also
 a realization of \(q\).  By total disintegration of \(q\), \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta b'\).  
 However, \( \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{ b \} ) = \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{a \} ) =\tau(\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{ b \} ))= \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K \cup \{b'\})\) so that \(b \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta b'\).  
 With this we conclude that \(b\) is a 
 \(\Delta\)-rational function \(f\) of \(a\).  
 The situation is symmetric between
 \(p\) and \(q\) and we see that \(a\) is
 a differential rational function \(g\) of \(b\). 

We see that there cannot be a second differential rational map \(h\) taking realizations of \(p\) to realizations of \(q\) for if there were, then \(\{b, h(g(b)) \}\) 
would be a set of two distinct realizations 
of \(q\) which are dependent over \(K\), 
contrary to total disintegration.  Likewise,
we cannot have another differential
rational function taking realizations
of \(q\) to realizations of \(p\).
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- definition-14: body_md -->
\label{def:strongly-minimal}
A definable set $X$ is \emph{strongly minimal} if
$X(\mathbb{U})$ is infinite
and every definable subset is either finite or its complement in 
\(X\) is finite.
<!-- end definition-14 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
The field of constants \(\mathsf{C} = \mathbb{U}^\Delta := \{ a \in \mathbb{U} :
\delta_i(a) = 0 \text{ for } 1 \leq i \leq m \}\) is a strongly minimal set
relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\).
Indeed, by quantifier elimination, the structure induced on \(\mathsf{C}\) by 
differential equations is just that of a pure algebraically closed field
of characteristic zero.  The definable subsets of \(\mathsf{C}\) being 
finite Boolean combinations of the zero sets of ordinary polynomials in one 
variable are all finite or cofinite.  On the other hand, if we consider 
\(\mathsf{C}\) relative to the theory \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\) it is \emph{not} strongly minimal.  
For example, the set defined by adjoining the difference equation 
\(\sigma(x) = x\) is infinite as is its complement in \(\mathsf{C}\).
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
In the case that $n = 1$ and $m = 1$, 
that is, we are considering 
ordinary differential equations in one variable,
it is easy to 
see that if \(K\) is algebraically closed and 
$P \in K\{x\}_\Delta$ is an irreducible 
$\Delta$-polynomial of order one (that is, $P = F(x,\delta_1 x)$ for some irreducible 
polynomial $F(u,v) \in K[u,v]$ with $\deg_v F > 0$), then the equation 
$P(x) = 0$ defines a strongly minimal set relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\).
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
\label{remark-partialtype}
It is easy to check that if \(X\) is a strongly minimal set defined over the \(\ast\)-field
\(K\), then the partial type \(p_{X,K}\) defined by \(x \in X\) and \(P(x) \neq 0\) for 
each \(P \in K \{x\}_\ast\) for which \(\{ a \in X(\mathbb{U}) : P(a) = 0 \}\) is
finite is minimal.  

In some cases of interest, the formula \(x \in X\) already 
defines a complete minimal type.  For example, this is true when we take 
\(K = \mathbb{Q}\), \(m = 1\), and \(X\) defined by \(\delta_1(x) = x^3 - x^2 \land x \neq 0 \land x \neq 1\).
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- definition-15: body_md -->
\label{def:strictly-minimal-set}
A definable set \(X\), defined over a parameter set \(A\), is \emph{strictly minimal} if it is strongly minimal, its induced structure is \(\aleph_0\)-categorical, meaning that for each \(n\) there are only finitely many \(A\)-definable subsets of \(X^n\), and \(X\) admits no   \(A\)-definable equivalence relations other than equality and the universal relation.
<!-- end definition-15 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lm-strictminimal}
Work in a sufficiently saturated model of $\mathrm{DCF}_{0,m}$.
Let $X$ be strictly minimal over $A$, and let $p_{X,A}$
be its generic type over $A$.
Then $p_{X,A}$ is isolated by the formula defining $X$
and is totally disintegrated.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Suppose that $Y$ is a nonempty proper $A$-definable subset
of $X$. Then
\[
E_Y(x,y)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\bigl(x\in Y \land y\in Y\bigr)
\lor
\bigl(x\notin Y \land y\notin Y\bigr)
\]
defines an equivalence relation on $X$ that is neither
equality nor the universal relation, contradicting strict
minimality. Thus $X$ has no nonempty proper $A$-definable
subset, so the formula defining $X$ isolates $p_{X,A}$.

 
Moreover, \(
X\cap\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(A)=\varnothing.
\)
Indeed, an element of this intersection would belong to
a nonempty finite $A$-definable subset of $X$, a contradiction.

We also use the fact that an $\aleph_0$-categorical strongly
minimal set in $\mathrm{DCF}_{0,m}$ is geometrically trivial (see~\cite[Lemma 2.23]{Nagloo–Pillay-2017} whose proof extends to 
several commuting derivations using~\cite[Corollary 4.2(ii)]{Pillay-foundational}). For $x,y\in X$, define
\[x\sim y
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
y\in\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(A,x).
\]
Since every element of $X$ is nonalgebraic over $A$,
 $\sim$ is an equivalence relation.  
By $\aleph_0$-categoricity, this  relation
is $A$-definable, and each of its classes
\(
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(A,x)\cap X
\)
is finite. As $X$ is infinite, $\sim$ is not the universal
relation. Strict minimality therefore implies that
$\sim$ is equality and for each $x\in X,$
\(\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(A,x)\cap X=\{x\}\). 
Since $p_{X,A}$ is geometrically trivial, pairwise independence of a set of distinct realizations implies its independence.
Thus,  $p_{X,A}$ is totally disintegrated.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm-trivialminimal}
Suppose $K$ is an algebraically closed  \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field.
Let $p \in S_{x,\Delta}(K)$ be a  minimal, trivial
type relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\). Then 
relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\), every completion of \(p\) 
is minimal as a quantifier-free type.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
Looking ahead to the discussion of Lascar rank in Section~\ref{sec:non-trivial-types} and the characterization of forking relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\), the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm-trivialminimal} may be rephrased by saying that every completion of \(p\) has \(\operatorname{SU}\)-rank one.
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:aut-preserves-minimality-and-triviality}
Let \(K\) be a sub-\(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field of
\(\mathbb{U}\), \(a\) a 
tuple from \(\mathbb{U}\), and
\(N \in \mathbb{Z}\) an integer.  
If \(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(a/K)\) is minimal, then so is \(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(\sigma^N(a)/K)\).
Likewise, if  \(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(a/K)\) is trivial, then so is \(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(\sigma^N(a)/K)\).
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
These invariance properties follow easily from the fact that \(\sigma\) preserves 
\(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta\).  Let us elaborate, considering first the minimality.
Let \(L\) be a \(\Delta\)-field extending \(K\) and suppose that 
\(\sigma^N(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).  Because \(\sigma\), and thus also \(\sigma^N\), is 
an automorphism of \(\mathbb{U}\) as a \(\Delta\)-field, we have 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta \sigma^{-N}(L)\).  Since \(\operatorname{tp}(a/K)\) is minimal, it must be 
that \(a\) is algebraic over \(\sigma^{-N}(L)\).  Applying 
\(\sigma^N\) and observing that \(\sigma^N\) preserves algebraicity, we see that 
\(\sigma^N(a)\) is algebraic over \(L\).  Likewise, because \(a\) is not algebraic over 
\(K\), neither is \(\sigma^N(a)\).  That is, we have demonstrated that \(\operatorname{tp}(\sigma^N(a)/K)\)
is minimal.

The argument for triviality is similar:  suppose that \(\{a_1, \ldots, a_n\}\) is a finite 
list of realizations of \(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(\sigma^N(a)/K)\) and that \(a_i \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta a_j\) for 
\(1 \leq i < j \leq n\).  Then \(\sigma^{-N}(a_i)\) is a realization of \(\operatorname{tp}(a/K)\) 
for each \(i\) and \(\sigma^{-N}(a_i) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta \sigma^{-N}(a_j)\) for 
\(1 \leq i < j \leq n\) as \(\sigma^{-N}\) respects \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta\).  Since
\(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(a/K)\) is trivial, the set \(\{ \sigma^{-N}(a_1), \ldots, 
\sigma^{-N}(a_n) \}\) is \(K\)-independent.  Applying \(\sigma^{N}\) we
see that \(\{a_1, \ldots, a_n\}\) is also \(K\)-independent, verifying that \(\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(\sigma^{N}(a)/K)\)
is trivial.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:independentsets-and-forking}
Let \(K \subseteq L\) be an extension of  \(\Delta\)-fields. 
Suppose that \(a_1, \ldots, a_n\) is a finite sequence of tuples from \(\mathbb{U}\) so that for each \(i\), 
\begin{itemize} 
\item \(\operatorname{tp}(a_i/K)\in S_{\Delta}(K)\) is trivial and minimal, and 
\item the tuple \(a_i\) is not algebraic over \(L\). 
\end{itemize}
Then \(a_1, \ldots, a_n \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\). If, in addition, \(\{a_1,\ldots,a_n\}\) is \(K\)-independent, then it is also \(L\)-independent.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We first argue by induction on \(n\) to show that \(a_1, \ldots, a_n \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).
As each  \(a_i\) is not algebraic over \(L\) and \(p_i=\operatorname{tp}(a_i/K)\in S_{\Delta}(K)\) is minimal, $a_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K L$. 
Let us consider the inductive case of \(k+1\).
Our inductive hypothesis is that \(a_1, \ldots, a_k \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\) for $1\leq k<n$.
There are two subcases to consider: \(a_{k+1} \in \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K 
\cup \{a_1, \ldots, a_k\})\) or  \(a_{k+1} \notin \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K 
\cup \{a_1, \ldots, a_k\})\).
In the first case, since independence is insensitive to taking algebraic
closures, we have that  \(a_1, \ldots, a_k \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\) implies
that \(\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K 
\cup \{a_1, \ldots, a_k\}) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\). Since in this 
case \(\{a_1, \ldots, a_k, a_{k+1}\} \subseteq \operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K 
\cup \{a_1, \ldots, a_k\})\), we conclude by monotonicity that  
\(a_1, \ldots, a_{k+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).  In the nonalgebraic case, since
\(p_{k+1}\) is minimal, we have \(a_{k+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta a_1, \ldots, a_k\).  
By the hypothesis of the lemma,   \(a_{k+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).
By our inductive hypothesis we have \(a_1, \ldots, a_k \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).
Thus, taking \(B_0 := \{ a_1, \ldots, a_k\}\) and \(B_1 := L\) in 
Lemma~\ref{lemma-trivialregular}, we conclude that 
\(a_{k+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K \{ a_1, \ldots, a_k \} \cup L\).   By transitivity, 
this implies that \(a_{k+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_{K \cup \{a_1, \ldots, a_k \}}^\Delta L\).
Using our inductive hypothesis that \(a_1, \ldots, a_k \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\) and 
transitivity again, we conclude \(a_1, \ldots, a_{k+1} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\), 
as required.  So by induction, we conclude that \(a_1, \ldots, a_{n} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).

Assume in addition that $\{a_1,\ldots,a_n\}$ is $K$-independent. 
Fix $i$, and let \(b:=(a_j)_{j\neq i}.\)
Then \(a_i,b\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L.\)
By symmetry and transitivity,
\(
a_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_{K\langle b\rangle_\Delta}^\Delta L.
\)
By the $K$-independence of the set $\{a_1,\ldots,a_n\}$,
$a_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^{\Delta}K\langle b\rangle_\Delta$.
By transitivity, \(a_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\langle b\rangle_\Delta.\)
By base monotonicity, \(a_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_L^\Delta L\langle b\rangle_\Delta\).
Thus, for every $i$,
\(
a_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_L  L\langle a_j:j\neq i\rangle_\Delta.
\)
Hence $\{a_1,\ldots,a_n\}$ is $L$-independent.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:still-non-algebraic}
Let \(K \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) be 
a small  \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field,
and let \(p\in S_\Delta(K)\) 
be a non-algebraic type.  Suppose that \(q\) is a
completion of \(p\) relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\).  Then 
\(q\) is also non-algebraic.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
If \(q\) were algebraic, then it would have a realization \(a\) in 
\(\operatorname{acl}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}(K)\). 
Since \(\operatorname{acl}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}(K)\) is the 
field theoretic algebraic closure of the inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field
generated by \(K\) and \(K\) is already an  inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field, we have \(\operatorname{acl}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}(K) = 
\operatorname{acl}^\Delta(K) = K^{\operatorname{alg}}\).  That is, 
the coordinates of \(a\) would lie in \(K^{\operatorname{alg}}\), meaning that 
\(p\) would be algebraic, contrary to our hypotheses.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Let $q$ be a completion of $p$ relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$.
By Lemma \ref{lem:still-non-algebraic}, $q$  is non-algebraic.
We need to show that if \(a\) is a realization of 
\(q\) and \(L\) is an algebraically closed \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field
extending \(K\) inside \(\mathbb{U}\), then 
either the coordinates of \(a\) come from \(L\) or 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} L\). 

Let us suppose towards a contradiction that 
\(a\) is not algebraic over \(L\) while 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} L\).
Recall that 
this dependence means that 
\(\langle K \cup a \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\)
and \(L\) are algebraically dependent over \(K\).
Using the facts that \(\langle K \cup a \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\) is 
the field generated by 
\(\bigcup_{j \in \mathbb{Z}} \sigma^j(\langle K \cup \{a \}\rangle_\Delta) \) and an algebraic 
dependence is witnessed by a single polynomial
relation, we can find some finite \(N\) and \(M\) so that 
\(K \langle \sigma^{-M}(a), \ldots, \sigma^{N}(a) \rangle_\Delta\) and \(L\)
are algebraically dependent over \(K\).  Since 
\(\sigma\) restricts to an automorphism of 
both \(K\) and \(L\) and \(\sigma\) respects 
algebraic (in)dependence, we may replace \(N\) with \(N+M\)
so that \(K \langle a, \sigma(a), \ldots, \sigma^N(a)\rangle_\Delta\) is algebraically dependent with \(L\)
over \(K\).

From the definition of \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta\), this dependence 
means that \(a, \sigma(a), \ldots, \sigma^N(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\)
which contradicts Lemma~\ref{lem:independentsets-and-forking} as 
each type \(\operatorname{tp}(\sigma^i(a)/K)\) is minimal and trivial
and no \(\sigma^i(a)\) is algebraic over \(L\) for 
\(i \in \mathbb{Z}\).
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- definition-16: body_md -->
\label{def:twisted-composition}
Let \(f\) be a \(\Delta\)-rational function and \(\tau:K \to K\) a 
\(\Delta\)-field automorphism.  We write \(f^\tau\) for the \(\Delta\)-rational function obtained by applying \(\tau\) to the coefficients of \(f\).  For \(j \in \mathbb{N}\)
we define \(f^{\diamond^\tau j}\) by recursion on \(j\): 
\[f^{\diamond^\tau 0} := f, \qquad  f^{\diamond^\tau j + 1} := f^{\tau^{j+1}} \circ f^{\diamond^\tau j}. \]
<!-- end definition-16 -->
<!-- remark-15: body_md -->
\label{rk-iteration-of-f}
The notation may seem somewhat unusual, but it will be useful when we study possible relations between a totally disintegrated type
$p=\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(a/K)$
and its transforms
$p^{\tau^j}:=\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}(\tau^j(a)/K)$
where \(\tau\) is an automorphism of \(\mathbb{U}\) as 
a \(\Delta\)-field.
Since \(p^{\tau^j}\) is also totally disintegrated for every $j$, Proposition~\ref{prop:dependence-totally-disintegrate} implies that, if
$p\not\perp p^\tau,$
then there exists a bijective \(\Delta\)-rational function
$f: p(\mathbb U)\longrightarrow p^\tau(\mathbb U).$
For each \(j\geq 0\), its transform \(f^{\tau^j}\) is a bijective $\Delta$-rational map
$f^{\tau^j}:\,p^{\tau^j}(\mathbb U)\longrightarrow p^{\tau^{j+1}}(\mathbb U).$
Thus, defining $f^{\diamond^\tau (j+1)}
:=f^{\tau^{j+1}}\circ f^{\diamond^\tau j},$
we obtain a bijective \(\Delta\)-rational function
$f^{\diamond^\tau j}:
p(\mathbb U)\longrightarrow p^{\tau^{j+1}}(\mathbb U).$
<!-- end remark-15 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:automatic-sigma-hypertranscendence}
Let $K$ be an algebraically closed
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-field, and let
$p\in S_{x,\Delta}(K)$ be a minimal type relative
to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$. Suppose that
\(p\perp p^{\sigma^i}\) for every  \(i>0.\)
Then, for every realization $a\models p$, the family
$(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in\mathbb Z}$ is independent over
$K$ relative to $\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta$. In particular, if $x$ is a
single variable, every realization of $p$ is
$\sigma$-transcendental over $K$.

Moreover, $p$ has a unique complete quantifier-free
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-extension over $K$, and this extension
is minimal relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$.
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Suppose towards a contradiction that there exists $a\models p$ such that the family
$(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in\mathbb Z}$ is not independent over
$K$ relative to $\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta$.   Choose such a realization $a$ so that the least positive integer
$\ell$ for which $a,\sigma(a),\ldots,\sigma^\ell(a)$ are $K$-dependent relative to $\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta$ is as small as possible.
Then we would have \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta \sigma(a),
\ldots, \sigma^{\ell-1} a\) and \(\sigma^\ell(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta \sigma(a),
\ldots, \sigma^{\ell-1} a \) as $\sigma(K)=K$,
whereas
\(
\sigma^\ell(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta a, \sigma(a),\ldots,\sigma^{\ell-1}(a).
\)
By transitivity and symmetry, this gives \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_{K \langle \sigma(a), \ldots, \sigma^{\ell-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta}^\Delta \sigma^{\ell}(a)\).  Hence, \(p \not \perp p^{\sigma^\ell}\),
contrary to our hypothesis. Thus, we have proved that for every realization $a\models p$, the family
$(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in\mathbb Z}$ is independent over
$K$ relative to $\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta$. In particular, if $a$ is a
single element in $\mathbb U$,  
 $a$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over $K$.


We now prove the assertion about quantifier-free type extensions.
Let $a\models p$. Every quantifier-free $\sigma$-$\Delta$-formula in $a$ can be written as a $\Delta$-formula in finitely
many transforms $(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in I}$ for some finite $I\subseteq\mathbb Z$.
By stationarity and the independence of $(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in I}$ proved above, 
$\operatorname{tp}_{\Delta}\left((\sigma^i(a))_{i\in I}/K\right)$ is uniquely determined by the individual types $p^{\sigma^i}$.
These types depend only on $p$ and the  action
of $\sigma$ on $K$. Hence all realizations of $p$
have the same quantifier-free
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-type over $K$, proving uniqueness
of the complete quantifier-free extension, say $q$.

To prove minimality, let $L\supseteq K$ be a small
algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\Delta$-field, and let
$a\models p$ be nonalgebraic over $L$. Set $p_L=\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/L)$.
 By minimality and  stationarity of $p^{\sigma^i}$,  
$p_L^{\sigma^i}=\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(\sigma^i(a)/L)$ is the
unique nonforking extension of $p^{\sigma^i}$ to $L$.
In particular,
\(
\sigma^i(a)\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K L\) for every \(i\in\mathbb Z.
\) 
Orthogonality is preserved under nonforking
extensions, so
\(p_L\perp p_L^{\sigma^i}\)  for every \(i>0.\)
By the independence assertion proved above with $L$ in place of $K$, we get
$(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in\mathbb Z}$ is independent over $L$.
Consequently, for every finite $I\subseteq\mathbb Z$,
the tuple $(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in I}$ is independent over
$L$. Since $\sigma^i(a)\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_{K}L$ for each $i$, by transitivity,
\(
(\sigma^i(a))_{i\in I}\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K L.
\)
By finite character and
\cite[Proposition~3.1(v)]{LS-DCFAm}, we  obtain
\(
a\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}_K L.
\)
This holds for arbitrary small
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-field extensions of $K$, by passing
to their algebraic closures.
This proves the minimality of $q$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:completions-totally-disintegrated}
Let \(p \in S_\Delta(K)\) be a totally disintegrated type over a small algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\Delta$-field $K$.
Let \(\ell \in \mathbb{Z}_+\) be the least positive integer 
for which \(p \not \perp p^{\sigma^\ell}\), and \(f\) be the  
\(\Delta\)-rational function over $K$ given by Proposition~\ref{prop:dependence-totally-disintegrate}
so that for \(a\) realizing \(p\) we have that \(f(a)\) realizes \(p^{\sigma^\ell}\). 
Then for any $a\in p(\mathbb U)$,
either 
\begin{itemize}
    \item[(1)] $a$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over $K$; or
    \item[(2)] $a$ satisfies the $\sigma$-equation  $\sigma^{\ell n}(x) = f^{\diamond^{\sigma^\ell}n-1}(x)$ for some $n\in\mathbb N_{>0}$.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Suppose \(a\) is not \(\sigma\)-transcendental. 
Then \(a\) is \(\sigma\)-algebraic.
Let \(A \in \mathbb{Z}_+\) be minimal so that \(a, \ldots, \sigma^{A} a\) are not \(K\)-independent relative to \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta\).
By triviality and minimality of 
\(p\), and hence also of \(p^{\sigma^i}\) for every \(i \in \mathbb{Z}\), this dependence yields \(\sigma^i(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta \sigma^j(a)\) for some \(0 \leq i < j \leq A\).  Applying \(\sigma^{-i}\) we may take \(i = 0\) and 
by minimality of \(A\), we have \(j = A\). That is, $\sigma^A(a)\in\operatorname{acl}(K,a)$ and $p\not \perp p^{\sigma^A}$.   By Proposition~\ref{prop:dependence-totally-disintegrate},
there is some bijective (on \(p\)) \(\Delta\)-rational function \(h\) taking realizations of \(p\) to realizations of \(p^{\sigma^A}\).
Since both $h(a)$ and $\sigma^A(a)$ are realizations of the totally disintegrated type $p^{\sigma^A}$ in $\operatorname{acl}(K,a)$ with $a$ a realization of a minimal type $p$, 
we have \(h(a) = \sigma^A(a)\).  

We claim that \(A\) is a multiple of \(\ell\). Since $A\geq \ell$, we can write \(A = \ell n + r\) with 
\(0 \leq r < \ell\) and \(n \in \mathbb{Z}_{+}\). Suppose towards
a contradiction that \(r \neq 0\).
By Remark \ref{rk-iteration-of-f}, $f^{\diamond^{\sigma^\ell} n-1}$ is a bijective \(\Delta\)-rational function from $p(\mathbb U)$ to $p^{\sigma^{\ell n}}(\mathbb U)$.
Let \(g\) be the \(\Delta\)-rational
function which is inverse to \(f^{\diamond^{\sigma^\ell} n-1}\) on \(p^{\sigma^{\ell n}}\) given by 
Proposition~\ref{prop:dependence-totally-disintegrate}.  
Then \((h \circ g)^{\sigma^{-\ell n}}\)
is a \(\Delta\)-rational function which takes realizations of \(p\) to 
realizations of \(p^{\sigma^{r}}\).  Hence, \(p \not \perp p^{\sigma^r}\).  As 
\(\ell\) was minimal with \(p \not \perp p^{\sigma^\ell}\), we see that we must have
had \(r = 0\). So $A=\ell n$.

From the uniqueness assertion in Proposition~\ref{prop:dependence-totally-disintegrate},  
we see that \(h = f^{\diamond^{\sigma^\ell} n-1}\).  That is, \(a\) satisfies \(\sigma^{\ell n}(x) = f^{\diamond^{\sigma^\ell}n-1}(x)\).
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{cor-strictdisintegrated-fixed}
Let $K$ be a  $\sigma$-$\Delta$-field with $K=K^{\sigma}:=\{x\in K: \sigma(x)=x\}$.
Let $p\in S_\Delta(K)$ be a totally  disintegrated type. Then for any $a\in p(\mathbb U)$, either $a$ is $\sigma$-transcendental or $a$ satisfies  $\sigma^\ell(a)=a$ for some $\ell \in\mathbb N_{>0}$.
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Since $K=K^{\sigma}$, $p^{\sigma^\ell}=p$ for each $\ell\in\mathbb N_{>0}$. 
Thus, if for some \(a\) realizing \(p\) we have a dependence 
amongst \(a, \sigma(a), \ldots, \sigma^N(a)\) for some \(N\), then 
we have a dependence amongst realizations of \(p\) itself.  Since
\(p\) is totally disintegrated, this would force 
\(\sigma^i(a) = \sigma^j(a)\) for some \(0 \leq i < j \leq N\).  Setting
\(\ell := j - i\), we would have \(\sigma^\ell(a) = a\).
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->
Let $p\in S_{\Delta}(\mathbb Q)$ be the generic type of $x'=x^3-x^2.$ By \cite[p.532, Corollary]{Rosenlicht} for \(n=2\) and \cite[p.160]{Kolchin-constrained} in general, if $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ are distinct non-algebraic solutions of $x'=x^3-x^2$, then $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ are algebraically independent over $\mathbb Q.$ 
Thus, $p$ is totally disintegrated.
For each $a\in p(\mathbb U)$, $\sigma^i(a)\in p(\mathbb U)$. Thus, either $a$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over $\mathbb Q$, or $a$ satisfies $\sigma^n(a)=a$ for some $n\in\mathbb N_{>0}$.
<!-- end example-4 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:omega-cat-dichotomy}
Let $K$ be a $\sigma$-$\Delta$-field such that
$K=K^{\sigma}$.
Let $p\in S_{x,\Delta}(K)$ be a minimal type in a single variable $x$ that is
$\aleph_0$-categorical over $K$.
For every $\alpha\in p(\mathbb U)$, exactly one of
the following holds:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $\alpha$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over $K$;
    \item $\sigma^N(\alpha)=\alpha$ for some integer $N\geq1$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let $D:=p(\mathbb U)$.
Since $\sigma$ fixes $K$ pointwise and commutes with
the derivations, $\sigma(D)=D$.
For every finite $B\subseteq D$, the set
\(
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K\cup B)\cap D
\)
is finite. Indeed, $\aleph_0$-categoricity implies that
there are only finitely many relatively
$(K\cup B)$-definable subsets of $D$.
Every element of \(\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K\cup B)\cap D\) belongs to a finite
such subset, so their union is finite.

Suppose that $\alpha$ is not $\sigma$-transcendental
over $K$. Then there exists $r\geq 1$ such that
\(\alpha,\sigma(\alpha),\ldots,\sigma^r(\alpha)
\)
are algebraically dependent over $K$, and hence dependent
over $K$ relative to $\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta$.
Write $b_i:=\sigma^i(\alpha)$, and choose the least
$\ell\geq1$ such that
\(b_0,b_1,\ldots,b_\ell
\)
is dependent over $K$ relative to $\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta$.
Such an $\ell$ exists and is positive because $p$
is nonalgebraic.

By the choice of $\ell$, the tuple
$(b_0,\ldots,b_{\ell-1})$ is independent over $K$.
Applying $\sigma$, we see that
$(b_1,\ldots,b_\ell)$ is also independent over $K$.
Since every $b_i$ realizes the minimal type $p$,
the dependence of the full tuple implies
\[
\begin{aligned}
b_\ell
&\in
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_0,\ldots,b_{\ell-1}),\\
b_0
&\in
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_1,\ldots,b_\ell).
\end{aligned}
\]
Consequently,
\[
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_0,\ldots,b_{\ell-1})
=
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_1,\ldots,b_\ell).
\]

Let
\(
F:=
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_0,\ldots,b_{\ell-1})
\cap D.
\)
The set $F$ is finite and contains $\alpha$.
Moreover,
\[
\sigma(F)
=
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_1,\ldots,b_\ell)
\cap D=
\operatorname{acl}^{\Delta}(K,b_0,\ldots,b_{\ell-1})
\cap D=F.
\]
Thus $\sigma$ permutes the finite set $F$, and hence
$\sigma^N(\alpha)=\alpha$ for some $N\geq1$.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- example-5: body_md -->
Consider the differential equation satisfied by the
classical $j$-function:
\[
\chi(y):=
\left(\frac{y''}{y'}\right)'
-\frac12\left(\frac{y''}{y'}\right)^2
+\frac{y^2-1968y+2654208}
       {2y^2(y-1728)^2}(y')^2
=0.
\]
By \cite{FS2018}, its solution set $X$ is strongly
minimal and geometrically trivial.

Fix $\lambda\in\mathbb C$, and let $\sigma$ act by
translation on the differential field generated
over $\mathbb C$ by the functions
$j(t+k\lambda)$, $k\in\mathbb Z$, interpreted as
meromorphic germs. Thus $\sigma$ fixes $\mathbb C$
pointwise, commutes with $\delta=d/dt$, and satisfies
\(\sigma^r(j(t))=j(t+r\lambda),\,\, 
 r\in\mathbb Z.
\)

\begin{enumerate}
\item
If $\lambda\notin\mathbb Q$, then $j(t)$ is
$\sigma$-transcendental over $\mathbb Q$.
 
Hence by Theorem \ref{thm-trivialminimal},  $j(t)$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over any $\sigma$-$\delta$-field extension of $\mathbb Q$ over which it is transcendental.
\item
If $\lambda=p/q\in\mathbb Q$, where
$p\in\mathbb Z$, $q\geq1$, and $\gcd(p,q)=1$,
then the identity $j(t+1)=j(t)$ gives
\(
\sigma^q(j(t))=j(t+p)=j(t).
\)
Moreover, each pair $j(t)$ and $\sigma^r(j(t))$
satisfies a modular relation
\(
\Phi_N\bigl(j(t),\sigma^r(j(t))\bigr)=0
\)
for an appropriate classical modular polynomial
$\Phi_N\in\mathbb Z[Y,Z]$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end example-5 -->
<!-- definition-17: body_md -->
\label{def:sets-differential-forms}
Let \(K \subseteq \mathsf{C}\) be a small algebraically closed field of constants. 
Let \(X\) be a smooth projective algebraic curve over \(K\) and 
let \(\omega\) be a nonzero rational 1-form on \(X\).  We set 
\(\Xi(X,\omega) := \{ a \in X(\mathbb{U}) :  \omega(a)(\delta a) = 1 \} \)
where, if \(a\) is a pole of \(\omega\), the equation 
\(\omega(a)(\delta a) = 1\) is taken to mean \(\delta a = 0\).
<!-- end definition-17 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\cite[Lemma 2.23]{HrIt}  \label{hrushovski2003-lm2.23}  Let $\omega$ be a rational $1$-form on $\mathbb{P}^1$, defined over a constant field $K$. Assume $\omega$ has at least two nonzero residues, and that no two distinct nonzero residues of $\omega$ are rational multiples of each other. Then $X = \Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1, \omega)$ is strictly minimal over $K$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lm-PGL2}
Let $X=\Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_1)$ 
and $Y=\Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_2)$ 
be two strictly minimal subsets living on $\mathbb P^1$,
where \(\omega_1\) and \(\omega_2\) are 
rational \(1\)-forms on $\mathbb P^1$, defined over a constant field $K$.
 Then $X$ and $Y$ are  non-orthogonal if and only if one
is the image of the other under an element of the definable group
$\operatorname{PGL}_2(\mathbb U)$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
If $X$ and $Y$ are  nonorthogonal, then
there is a relatively Kolchin closed subset 
\(Z \subseteq \Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_1) \times \Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_2)\) 
which projects dominantly and finite-to-one in both directions.  
We may take \(Z\) to be irreducible.  By strict minimality, 
we may take \(Z\) to be defined over the same 
algebraically closed set of parameters as \(X\) and \(Y\).
From strict minimality of these sets, \(Z\) must be the graph of a bijection as if 
\(a, b \in \Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_1)\) and \(c \in \Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_2)\)
with \((a,c) \in Z\) and \((b,c) \in Z\), then \(b\) would lie in a 
finite set definable from \(a\), which by strict minimality can consist only of 
\(a\) itself.  Hence, \(Z\) is a single-rooted relation. 
Reasoning with the 
two factors reversed, we see that \(Z\) is the graph of a function.  
In general, a definable function is piecewise differential rational.
By strong  minimality of $X$, one piece is defined on a cofinite definable subset of $X$ and the remaining pieces consist of algebraic points. 
As $X$ is strictly minimal, $X$ has no algebraic points, and thus the definable function is a single differential rational function. Since the derivative of any point in \(\Xi^*(\mathbb{P}^1,\omega_1)\) is 
rational over that point, it must be that \(Z\) is the graph of an invertible rational function, that is, of a rational automorphism of \(\mathbb{P}^1\): an element of \(\operatorname{PGL}_2(\mathbb{U})\).
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lemma-strictlyminimal}
Let $K$ be an algebraically closed $\delta$-field of characteristic
zero. Let $f_1, f_2\in C_K[y]$ have the same degree $n\geq 3$, and for $i=1,2$, write
\(
f_i(y)=f_{i,n}y^n+f_{i,n-1}y^{n-1}+\cdots+f_{i,0}
\).
Let $p_1$ and $p_2$ be the generic types over $K$ of 
\[
V_1^*=\{y:\delta y=f_1(y),\ f_1(y)\neq 0\},
\qquad
V_2^*=\{y:\delta y=f_2(y),\ f_2(y)\neq 0\},
\]
respectively, and suppose that they are strictly minimal.
Then $p_1$ and $p_2$ are nonorthogonal if and only if there exist
$a\in C_K^\times$ and $b\in C_K$ with $a^{n-1}=\frac{f_{1,n}}{f_{2,n}}$ such that
\[f_2(ay+b)=a f_1(y).
\]
In particular,
\(
b=\frac{1}{n}\left(
      a\frac{f_{1,n-1}}{f_{1,n}}-\frac{f_{2,n-1}}{f_{2,n}}
   \right).
\)
Moreover, if $u\models p_1$, $v\models p_2$, and $u\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\delta v$, then such $a,b$ can be chosen with $v=au+b$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
By Lemma~\ref{lm-PGL2},  $p_1$ and $p_2$ are nonorthogonal if and only if there is a M\"obius transformation
\(
\phi(y)=\frac{\alpha y+\beta}{\gamma y+\eta}\) with $\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \eta\in \mathbb U$ and
\(\alpha\eta-\beta\gamma\neq 0,
\)
which carries a generic solution of $\delta y=f_1(y)$ to a generic
solution of $\delta z=f_2(z)$. Take a solution $y$ of $\delta y=f_1(y)$ transcendental over $E:=K\langle \alpha, \beta, \gamma, \eta\rangle$.
Differentiating $\phi(y)$ and clearing denominators gives the following
identity in $E[y]$:
\[
\begin{aligned}
& (\gamma y+\eta)^{n-2}
  \Bigl[
  \bigl((\delta\alpha)y+\delta\beta+\alpha f_1(y)\bigr)
       (\gamma y+\eta) \\
&\hspace{35mm}
  - (\alpha y+\beta)
    \bigl((\delta\gamma)y+\delta\eta+\gamma f_1(y)\bigr)
  \Bigr] \\
&\hspace{15mm}=
  (\gamma y+\eta)^n
  f_2\!\left(\frac{\alpha y+\beta}{\gamma y+\eta}\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
The expression in brackets is
$(\alpha\eta-\beta\gamma)f_1(y)+R(y)$, where $\deg R\leq 2$.
If $\gamma\neq 0$, the left-hand side therefore has degree $2n-2$
and leading coefficient
$f_{1,n}\gamma^{n-2}(\alpha\eta-\beta\gamma)\neq 0$.
The right-hand side has degree at most $n$.
Since $2n-2>n$, this is impossible. Hence $\gamma=0$.

Set $a=\alpha/\eta$ and $b=\beta/\eta$.
The above identity now becomes
\[
a f_1(y)+(\delta a)y+\delta b=f_2(ay+b).
\]
Comparing coefficients of $y^n$ gives
$a^{n-1}=f_{1,n}/f_{2,n}$. Since $K$ is algebraically closed and
$f_{1,n}/f_{2,n}\in C_K$, we have $a\in C_K^\times$.
Comparing coefficients of $y^{n-1}$ gives
\[
a f_{1,n-1}=a^{n-1}(n f_{2,n} b+f_{2,n-1}),
\]
and therefore
\[
b=\frac{1}{n}\left(
      a\frac{f_{1,n-1}}{f_{1,n}}-\frac{f_{2,n-1}}{f_{2,n}}
   \right)\in C_K.
\]
Thus $\delta a=\delta b=0$ and $f_2(ay+b)=a f_1(y)$.


Now suppose that $u\models p_1$, $v\models p_2$, and
$u\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\delta v$. Minimality gives
\[
\operatorname{acl}^\delta(K,u)
=\operatorname{acl}^\delta(K,v)
\] by Remark \ref{rk:alg-non-orthogonal}.
For an affine bijection $\phi(y)=ay+b$ as above, $\phi(u)\models p_2$
and
\[
\operatorname{acl}^\delta(K,\phi(u))
=\operatorname{acl}^\delta(K,u)
=\operatorname{acl}^\delta(K,v).
\]
Strict minimality of $p_2$ therefore implies $v=\phi(u)$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm-strictmin}
Let $K$ be an algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\delta$-field of
characteristic zero, and let
\(f(y)=y^n+f_{n-1}y^{n-1}+\cdots+f_0\in C_K[y],
n\geq 3.
\) 
Suppose that the generic type in $S_\delta(K)$ of
\(
V_f^*=\{y:\delta y=f(y),\ f(y)\neq 0\}
\)
is strictly minimal. Let $\xi$ be a solution of
$\delta y=f(y)$ transcendental over $K$.
Then either $\xi$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over $K$, or there exist
$s\geq 1$, $a\in(C_K)^\times$, and $b\in C_K$ such that
\[
\sigma^s(\xi)=a\xi+b,
\]
and
\[
f^{\sigma^s}(y)
=a f\!\left(\frac{y-b}{a}\right).
\]
In the latter case, 
 $a^{n-1}=1$ and $b=\frac{a f_{n-1}-\sigma^s(f_{n-1})}{n}.
$
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Suppose that $\xi$ is $\sigma$-algebraic over $K$.
Since $\xi$ is transcendental over $K$, there is an integer $k\geq 1$
such that $\xi,\sigma(\xi),\ldots,\sigma^k(\xi)$ are algebraically
dependent over $K$.
For $i\geq 0$, write
\[
\xi_i=\sigma^i(\xi),
\qquad
p_i=\operatorname{tp}_\delta(\xi_i/K).
\]
As $\sigma(K)=K$ and $\sigma$ commutes with $\delta$, each $p_i$
is the strictly minimal generic type of
$\delta y=f^{\sigma^i}(y)$ over $K$.
These types are geometrically trivial. Hence
Proposition~\ref{prop:indep-trivial} yields indices
$0\leq i<j\leq k$ such that
$\xi_i\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\delta\xi_j$.
This dependence is also ordinary algebraic dependence over $K$, for $\delta\xi_r=f^{\sigma^r}(\xi_r)$ implies 
\(K\langle\xi_r\rangle_\delta=K(\xi_r)\).
Thus there exists a nonzero $G\in K[y,z]$ such that $G(\xi_i,\xi_j)=0$.


Set $s=j-i\geq 1$. Applying $\sigma^{-i}$ gives
\(
G^{\sigma^{-i}}(\xi,\sigma^s(\xi))=0\),
where $G^{\sigma^{-i}}\in K[y,z]\setminus\{0\}$.
Since both $\xi$ and $\sigma^s(\xi)$ are transcendental over $K$,  $p_0$ and $p_s$
are not weakly orthogonal.
Apply the final assertion of Lemma~\ref{lemma-strictlyminimal}
to this particular pair of realizations, with $f_1=f$ and $f_2=f^{\sigma^s}$.
Since both polynomials are monic of degree $n$, it yields
$a\in(K^\delta)^\times$ and $b\in K^\delta$ with the desired form satisfying
\[
a^{n-1}=1,
\qquad
\sigma^s(\xi)=a\xi+b,
\qquad
f^{\sigma^s}(a y+b)=a f(y).
\]
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\cite{Rosenlicht} \label{Rosenlicht}
  Let $K$ be a differential field with constant field $C_K$, 
  and let $C_K(y)$ be a pure transcendental extension field of $C_K$. 
 Let $f(y)\in C_K(y)\backslash\{0\}$ and consider the differential equation $y' = f(y)$.  Suppose that $ \frac{1}{f}$ is neither of the forms  
\begin{equation} \label{eq-partialfraction}
\quad \frac{\partial g(y)}{\partial y}, \quad \text{or} \quad c\cdot\frac{\frac{\partial h(y)}{\partial y}}{h(y)},
\end{equation}
 where $g \in C_K(y)$, $c \in C_K^{\operatorname{alg}}$ and $h \in C_K^{\operatorname{alg}}(y)^\times$. 
Then the generic type of \( y' = f(y) \) is orthogonal to the constants.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{prop-rationalsolresidue}
The following criteria hold.\begin{itemize}
    \item[(1)] 
(\cite[Proposition 2.1]{vanderput2001})
The differential equation $\frac{\partial g(y)}{\partial y} = f(y)$  has a nonzero rational  solution $g(y)\in K(y)$ if and only if all the residues of $f(y)$ are zero.

\item[(2)]
 (\cite[Proposition 3.1]{bostan2024}) The logarithmic differential equation
\(\frac{\partial g(y)/\partial y}{g(y)}=f(y)\)
admits a nonzero solution \(g(y)\in K(y)\)  if and only if  $f(y)$ has at most a simple pole with integral residue at each point of $K^{\text{alg}}$ and vanishes at infinity.
 \end{itemize}
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{internal-squarefree}
