Let $K$ be a field of constants and let $f\in K[y]$ be a square-free polynomial of degree $d\geq 3$. 
Let $r_1,\ldots,r_d$ be the residues of $1/f$ at the zeros of $f$.
Suppose there exists $i\neq j$ such that $r_i/r_j\notin\mathbb Q$. 
Then the generic type of $y'=f(y)$ is orthogonal to the constants.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{Rosenlicht},
it suffices to show that the differential equations $\frac{\partial g}{\partial y}=\frac{1}{f(y)}$ and $\frac{\partial h(y)}{\partial y}=\frac{c}{f}\cdot h(y)$ for all $c\in K^{\operatorname{alg}}\backslash\{0\}$
do not have nonzero solutions in $K^{\operatorname{alg}}(y)$. 
Assume the solutions of $f(y)=0$ are $\beta_1,\ldots, \beta_d$.
Since $f$ is square-free,  $\beta_i\neq\beta_j$ for $i\neq j$.
So $r_i:=\text{res}(1/f,\beta_i)=\frac{1}{f'(\beta_i)}$ and $\text{res}(c/f,\beta_i)=cr_i$.
 Since $r_i\neq0$, by Proposition \ref{prop-rationalsolresidue} (1),
 $\frac{\partial g}{\partial y}=\frac{1}{f(y)}$ does not have a rational function solution over $K$ (or equivalently over $K^{\operatorname{alg}}$).
 It remains to show there does not exist nonzero $c\in K^{\operatorname{alg}}$ such that $cr_i\in\mathbb Z$ for every $i=1,\ldots,d$. Suppose the contrary. Then for any pair $(i,j)$, $r_i/r_j\in\mathbb Q$, a contradiction to the hypothesis. Thus, by Proposition \ref{prop-rationalsolresidue} (2) and Theorem \ref{Rosenlicht}, the generic type of $y'=f(y)$ is orthogonal
to the constants.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{residue-irreduciblepoly}
Let $K$ be a field of constants 
of characteristic zero, and let
$f\in K[y]$ have degree $n\geq 3$.
Let $K_f$ be the subfield generated over $\mathbb Q$ by the
coefficients of $f$. If $f$ is irreducible over $K_f$, then the 
generic  type 
 of $\delta y=f(y)$ in $S_\delta(K)$ is
orthogonal to the constants.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Let $L\supset K_f$ be a splitting field of $f$, and write
\[
f(y)=\lambda\prod_{i=1}^n(y-\beta_i),
\qquad \lambda=\operatorname{lc}(f).
\]
Since $f$ is irreducible over $K_f$, its roots
are distinct and the residues of $1/f$ at these roots are
\[
r_i=\frac{1}{f'(\beta_i)}\in L^\times
\qquad (1\leq i\leq n),
\]
where $f'(y)=\frac{\partial f(y)}{\partial y}$.
 By Lemma \ref{internal-squarefree},
 it suffices to show that there exists $i\neq j$ such that $r_i/r_j\notin \mathbb Q$.
 
Suppose, towards a contradiction, that all these ratios lie in
$\mathbb Q$.
Since the Galois group $\operatorname{Aut}(L/K_f)$ acts transitively on the roots of $f$,
for each $i$, there exists $\tau_i\in \operatorname{Aut}(L/K_f)$ such that $\tau_i(\beta_1)=\beta_i$.
So \[r_i=\frac{1}{f'(\beta_i)}=\tau_i\big(\frac{1}{f'(\beta_1)}\big)=\tau_i(r_1)=q_{i1}r_1, \quad \text{for some } q_{i1}\in\mathbb Q.\]
Set $N:=|\operatorname{Aut}(L/K_f)|$. Then $r_1=\tau_i^N(r_1)=q_{i1}^Nr_1$ and $q_{i1}^N=1$.
As $q_{i1}\in\mathbb Q$, $q_{i1}=\pm 1$ and $r_i=\pm r_1$ for each $i$. 
Let $$I_1:=\{i:\,r_i=r_1\}, \qquad I_2:=\{i:\,r_i=-r_1\}.$$
So the partial fraction decomposition of $1/f$ over $L$ is $$\frac{1}{f}=\sum_{i\in I_1}\frac{r_1}{y-\beta_i}-\sum_{i\in I_2}\frac{r_1}{y-\beta_i}.$$
Let $P(y)=\prod_{i\in I_1}(y-\beta_i)\in L[y]$ and $Q(y)=\prod_{i\in I_2}(y-\beta_i)\in L[y]$, where an empty product is $1$. Then $P(y)$ and $Q(y)$ are coprime with $f=\lambda P(y)Q(y)$.
So we have $$\frac{1}{f}=r_1\frac{\partial P(y)/\partial y}{P(y)}-r_1\frac{\partial Q(y)/\partial y}{Q(y)}=r_1\frac{\lambda}{f}\cdot \left(\frac{\partial P(y)}{\partial y} Q-P\frac{\partial Q(y)}{\partial y}\right).$$
So $\frac{\partial P(y)}{\partial y} Q-P\frac{\partial Q(y)}{\partial y}=1/(r_1\lambda)\in L$. 


If $I_2=\varnothing$, then $Q=1$ and $\deg P=n\geq 3$,
so $P'$ cannot be of degree zero. Thus this case is impossible.
Set $n_1=\deg P$ and $n_2=\deg Q$. If $n_1\neq n_2$,
then $P'Q-PQ'$ has leading coefficient $n_1-n_2\neq 0$ and degree
\[
n_1+n_2-1=n-1\geq 2,
\]
again a contradiction.
It remains to consider $n_1=n_2=k$. Then $n=2k\geq 3$, so $k\geq 2$.
Put $D=Q(y)-P(y)$. As $P$ and $Q$ are monic, $\deg(D)<k$ whenever $D\neq 0$.
If $D=0$, then $P'Q-PQ'=0$,  contradicting $P'Q-PQ'=1/(r_1\lambda)\neq0$. 
Otherwise, let $d=\deg D<k$. We have
$P'Q-PQ'=P'D-PD'.$
Its leading coefficient is $(k-d)\operatorname{lc}(D)\neq 0$,
and its degree is $k+d-1\geq 1$.
It therefore cannot be a nonzero element in $L$.
Thus, we get a contradiction. This proves the claim that there always exists $i\neq j$ such that $r_i/r_j\notin\mathbb Q$.  
By Lemma \ref{internal-squarefree},
the generic type is orthogonal to the constants.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{residue-cubic}
 Let $K$ be a $\delta$-field and let $f\in C_K[y]$ be a cubic polynomial irreducible over its field of definition $K_f$. 
 Let $r_1,r_2,r_3$ be the residues of $1/f$ at the zeros of $f$. 
 Then 
 \begin{itemize}
     \item the residues of $dy/f(y)$ at distinct poles are not rational
multiples of one another; equivalently, $r_i/r_j\notin\mathbb Q$ for all $1\leq i\neq j\leq 3$.
     \item The generic type of $y'=f(y)$ is  strictly minimal.
 \end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Scaling by the leading coefficient, we may assume that \(f\) is monic.
 Let $f=y^3+cy^2+by+d=(y-\alpha_1)(y-\alpha_2)(y-\alpha_3)$, where $\alpha_i\in K_f^{\text{alg}}$.
 Then $r_i:=\text{res}(1/f,\alpha_i)=1/f'(\alpha_i)$.
 Suppose on the contrary that there exists $i\neq j$ such that $r_i/r_j\in\mathbb Q$.
 Without loss of generality, suppose $r_1/r_2=-\frac{\alpha_2-\alpha_3}{\alpha_1-\alpha_3}=q\in\mathbb Q$.
 Since we also have $\alpha_1+\alpha_2+\alpha_3=-c$, $\alpha_2=q_1\alpha_3+c(q_1-1)/3$, where $q_1=(1+2q)/(1-q)\in\mathbb Q$ (here $q\neq1$, for otherwise, $\alpha_3=-c/3\in \mathbb Q(c)$).
 By taking the powers of $\alpha_2$ up to degree 3 and adding them together with corresponding coefficients of $f$, we get 
 \begin{eqnarray}
     0 &=&\alpha_2^3+c\alpha_2^2+b\alpha_2+d-q_1^3f(\alpha_3) \nonumber \\
 &=& q_1(q_1^2-1)(-b+c^2/3)\cdot\alpha_3\nonumber \\
 & & +[(q_1-1)^3c^3/27+(q_1-1)^2c^3/9+(q_1-1)bc/3+d-dq_1^3]. \nonumber
 \end{eqnarray}
Thus, $\alpha_3$ satisfies a linear polynomial, which must be 
the zero polynomial, by irreducibility of $f$. 
Note that $q_1\neq0$, or $1$, or $-1$, for otherwise, $\alpha_2=-c/3\in\mathbb Q(c)$, or $\alpha_2=\alpha_3$, or $\alpha_1=-c/3\in\mathbb Q(c)$ respectively, contradicting the irreducibility of $f$. So $b=c^2/3$.
Substituting $b=c^2/3$ into the degree zero term of the above equation, we get $(1-q_1^3)(d-c^3/27)=0$.
As $q_1\in\mathbb Q$ and $q_1\neq1$,  $d=c^3/27$.
So we have $f=y^3+cy^2+c^2y/3+c^3/27=(y+c/3)^3$, a contradiction to the irreducibility of $f$. Thus, no two distinct residues $r_i$, $r_j$ are rational multiples of each other. By Lemma \ref{hrushovski2003-lm2.23}, the generic type of $y'=f(y)$  is strictly minimal.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{cor-pgl3}
Let $K$ be a $\sigma$-$\delta$-field of characteristic zero,
and let \(f(y)=y^3+a\) with $a\in K^\delta\backslash\{0\}$. Set
$f^\sigma(y):=y^3+\sigma(a)$, and consider
\[
V:=\Xi^*\left(\mathbb P^1,\frac{dy}{f(y)}\right),
\qquad
V^\sigma:=\Xi^*\left(\mathbb P^1,
                    \frac{dy}{f^\sigma(y)}\right).
\]
 Then 
\begin{enumerate}
    \item Both $V$ and $V^\sigma$ are strictly minimal
    over the constant field $k:=\mathbb Q(a,\sigma(a))$,
    relative to $\mathrm{DCF}_0$.
    \item $V$ and $V^\sigma$ are non-orthogonal if and
    only if $\sigma(a)=\pm a$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Choose $\beta\in\mathbb U$ with $\beta^3=-a$, and let
$\omega$ be a primitive cube root of unity.
Since $a\neq0$, 
\(\beta, \beta\omega\) and \(\beta\omega^2\)
are all the simple poles of $dy/f(y)$ and it has no other poles. Its residues are
\[
r_j
:=
\operatorname{res}\left(\frac{1}{f(y)}, \beta\omega^j\right)
=
\frac{1}{3\beta^2\omega^{2j}},
\quad j=0,1,2.
\]
For distinct $i,j\in\{0,1,2\}$, we have
\(
\frac{r_i}{r_j}
=
\omega^{2(j-i)}
\in\{\omega,\omega^2\}
\subseteq\mathbb U\setminus\mathbb Q.
\)
Thus no two residues are rational multiples of one another.
By Lemma~\ref{hrushovski2003-lm2.23}, $V$ is strictly
minimal over $k$.
Since \(
\sigma(a)\neq0\) and \(\delta(\sigma(a))=0,
\) the same argument applies to $V^\sigma$.

 
 
Applying Lemma~\ref{lemma-strictlyminimal} over $k^{\operatorname{alg}}$ to $V$ and $V^\sigma$, we have $V$ and $V^\sigma$ are non-orthogonal if and only if 
there exists $\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}$ 
such that
\(
f^\sigma(\varepsilon y)
=
\varepsilon
f(y),
\)
equivalently,
\(
\sigma(a)=\varepsilon a.
\)
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
Let \(K\) be an algebraically closed
\(\sigma\)-\(\delta\)-field of characteristic zero, and let
\(a\in K^\delta\smallsetminus\{0\}\).
Let \(L\) be a \(\sigma\)-\(\delta\)-field extending \(K\),
and let \(\xi\) be a solution of
\(
\delta y=y^3+a
\)
that is transcendental over \(L\).
Then exactly one of the following holds:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \(\xi\) is \(\sigma\)-transcendental over \(L\);
    \item there exist \(n\in\mathbb N_{>0}\) and
    \(\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}\) such that
    \[
    \sigma^n(\xi)=\varepsilon\xi.
    \]
    In this case, necessarily
    \(\sigma^n(a)=\varepsilon a\).
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Set \(M=L^{\mathrm{alg}}\subset\mathbb U\). Then \(M\) is an algebraically closed \(\sigma\)-\(\delta\)-field, and \(\xi\) remains transcendental over \(M\). For each \(i\geq 0\), let
\[
W_i=\mathbb V\bigl(\delta y-y^3-\sigma^i(a)\bigr),
\]
and let \(p_i\in S_{\delta}(K)\) be its generic type over \(K\).
By Lemma~\ref{cor-pgl3}, each \(p_i\) is strictly minimal relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_0$. Hence, by
Lemma~\ref{lm-strictminimal} and the remark following it, each \(p_i\) is totally disintegrated.

Since \(\xi\) is transcendental over \(M\),
\(\operatorname{tp}_\delta(\xi/M)\) is a  nonforking extension of $p_0$ over $M$. Minimality of \(p_0\) therefore gives
\(\xi\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\delta M.
\)
Applying Theorem~\ref{thm-trivialminimal} over  \(K=K^{\operatorname{alg}}\subseteq M\), we obtain
\(
\xi\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^{\sigma\text{-}\delta}M.
\)
By Proposition~\ref{prop-nonforking-ideal},
\(I_{\sigma\text{-}\delta}(\xi/M)
= I_{\sigma\text{-}\delta}(\xi/K).
\)
In particular, for every \(r\geq 0\), the entries of \(
\bigl(\xi,\sigma(\xi),\ldots,\sigma^r(\xi)\bigr)\)
are algebraically independent over \(M\) if and only if they are algebraically independent over \(K\).
Thus \(\xi\) is \(\sigma\)-transcendental over \(M\) if and
only if it is \(\sigma\)-transcendental over \(K\).

Suppose that \(\xi\) is not \(\sigma\)-transcendental over
\(L\). Then it is \(\sigma\)-algebraic over \(M\), and hence, is \(\sigma\)-algebraic over \(K\).
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm-strictmin} to \(f(y)=y^3+a\).
There exist \(n\geq 1\), \(\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}\), and
\(b\in K^\delta\) such that
\[
\sigma^n(\xi)=\varepsilon\xi+b
\]
and
\[
y^3+\sigma^n(a)
=
\varepsilon f\!\left(\frac{y-b}{\varepsilon}\right)
=
(y-b)^3+\varepsilon a.
\]
Comparing coefficients of \(y^2\) and the constant terms on both sides  gives \(b=0\) and
\(\sigma^n(a)=\varepsilon a\).
Consequently, 
\(
\sigma^n(\xi)=\varepsilon\xi,
\)
as required.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- cor-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:irreducible-cubic-difference}
Let $K$ be an algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\delta$-field of
characteristic zero, and let
\[
f(y)=y^3+a_2y^2+a_1y+a_0\in C_K[y]
\]
be irreducible over $\mathbb Q(a_0,a_1,a_2)$.
Let $L\supseteq K$ be any $\sigma$-$\delta$-field  and let $\xi$
be a solution of $\delta y=f(y)$ with $\xi\notin L^{\mathrm{alg}}$.
Then either $\xi$ is $\sigma$-transcendental over $L$, or there exist
$s\geq 1$, $\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}$, and $b=\frac{\varepsilon a_2-\sigma^s(a_2)}{3} \in C_K$ such that
\[
\sigma^s(\xi)=\varepsilon\xi+b.
\]
In the second case, necessarily,
$f^{\sigma^s}(y)=\varepsilon f\bigl(\varepsilon(y-b)\bigr)$.
<!-- end cor-3 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Set $M=L^{\mathrm{alg}}\subset\mathbb U$. Then $M$ is an algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\delta$-field
containing $K$, and $\xi$ is transcendental over $M$.
By Lemma~\ref{residue-cubic}, the nonconstant solution locus
of $\delta y=f(y)$ has a strictly minimal generic type.
Its generic type over $M$ is a nonforking extension and is again
totally disintegrated by  Corollary~\ref{cor:trivial-field-of-definition-of-dependence}.

Suppose that $\xi$ is $\sigma$-algebraic over $L$.
It is then $\sigma$-algebraic over $M$, so
Theorem~\ref{thm-strictmin}, applied over $M$, yields
$s\geq 1$, $\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}$, and $b\in C_M$ such that
\[
\sigma^s(\xi)=\varepsilon\xi+b,
\qquad
f^{\sigma^s}(y)
=\varepsilon f\bigl(\varepsilon(y-b)\bigr).
\]
Comparing coefficients of $y^2$ gives
\[
\sigma^s(a_2)=\varepsilon a_2-3b.
\]
Hence $b=(\varepsilon a_2-\sigma^s(a_2))/3\in C_K$.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- remark-16: body_md -->
\label{rk:SU-rank-DCFA}
Fix a completion $T$ of $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$ and a monster model
$\mathbb U$ of $T$.
By \cite[Proposition~3.1(v)]{LS-DCFAm}, $T$ is
supersimple, so every complete type in finitely many
variables has ordinal-valued $\operatorname{SU}$-rank
\cite[Proposition~6.9(ii)]{KimPillay1997}.
In this section, $\operatorname{SU}(a/K)$ denotes the rank of the
complete first-order type $\operatorname{tp}(a/K)$
relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$.

For a small $\sigma$-$\Delta$-subfield $K$ of $\mathbb U$
and a finite tuple $a$, write
\(
K\langle a\rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}
=
K\bigl\langle\sigma^j(a)
:\ j\in\mathbb Z
\bigr\rangle_{\Delta}.
\)
By \cite[Proposition~3.1(v)]{LS-DCFAm}, for every small
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-field $L$ extending $K$,
\[
a\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}L
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
K\langle a\rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}
\text{ and }L
\text{ are algebraically disjoint over }K.
\]
Consequently, $\operatorname{SU}(a/K)\geq0$, and
\[
\operatorname{SU}(a/K)\geq\alpha+1
\]
if and only if there is a small
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-field $L\supseteq K$ such that
$\operatorname{SU}(a/L)\geq\alpha$ and
$K\langle a\rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}$ and $L$
are not algebraically disjoint over $K$.
For a nonzero limit ordinal $\lambda$,
$\operatorname{SU}(a/K)\geq\lambda$ if and only if
$\operatorname{SU}(a/K)\geq\alpha$ for every
$\alpha<\lambda$.
<!-- end remark-16 -->
<!-- definition-18: body_md -->
\label{def:sigma-degree}
We define the \(\sigma\)-order 
of \(a\) over \(K\), \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/K)\),
to be 
\begin{itemize}
    \item \(0\) if \(a\) is algebraic over \(K\),
    \item \(\ell \in \mathbb{Z}_+\) if \(\ell\) is 
    minimal with \(K \langle a \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} 
\subseteq K \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{\ell-1} a\rangle_\Delta^{\operatorname{alg}}\), and
\item \(\infty\) otherwise.
\end{itemize}
For a partial type \(\Sigma\) defined over 
\(K\) we define \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(\Sigma) := \sup \{ \operatorname{ord}_\sigma (a/K) : a \in \Sigma(\mathbb{U}) \} \).
<!-- end definition-18 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:fork-minimal-finite}
If \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)\) is 
minimal and \(L \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) is 
a small inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-extension of \(K\)
with \(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}_K L\), then 
\(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/L) < \infty\).
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
By definition of \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\), we have that 
\( (\sigma^j(a))_{j \in \mathbb{Z}} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K L\).  
By finite character of dependence, there are \(M,N \in \mathbb{N}\)
so that \((\sigma^j(a))_{-M \leq j \leq N} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).  Applying
\(\sigma^M\) and using the fact that \(K\) and \(L\) are 
inversive, we may assume that \(M = 0\).  Minimize  \(N\). If \(N = 0\), then \(a \in L^{\operatorname{alg}}\) which implies because \(L\) is inversive that \(\sigma^i(a)\) is algebraic over \(L\) for every \(i \in \mathbb{Z}\). That is, \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/L) = 0\).  Consider now the case that \(N > 0\).
We have that \(a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K L\)  so 
that by transitivity \(\sigma^N(a) \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_{K \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta} L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta \).  Since \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)\) 
is minimal, so is \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(\sigma^N(a)/K)\).
Hence, this dependence gives that 
\(\sigma^N(a)\) is algebraic over 
\(L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta\).  
Iterating \(\sigma\), we see that \[L \langle \{ \sigma^j(a) 
: j \in \mathbb{N} \} \rangle_\Delta 
\subseteq L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta^{\operatorname{alg}}.\]  


 Applying $\sigma^{-1}$ to the above gives $\sigma^{N-1}(a)\in L\langle\sigma^{-1}(a),a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-2}(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}$. We claim that $\sigma^{N-1}(a)\notin L\langle a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-2}(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}$.
 Otherwise, since $a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-1}(a)\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K L$, we would get $\sigma^{N-1}(a)\in K\langle a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-2}(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}$.  Applying $\sigma$, we get  $\sigma^{N}(a)\in K\langle \sigma(a),\ldots,\sigma^{N-1}(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}$, whence $ K\langle a,\ldots,\sigma^N(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}=K\langle a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-1}(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}$ would be independent from $L$ over $K$, a contradiction to $a,\ldots,\sigma^{N}(a)\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_K L$. This proves the claim.
 Thus, we have $\sigma^{N-1}(a)\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^\Delta_{L\langle a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-2}(a)\rangle_\Delta} \sigma^{-1}(a)$.
 By symmetry and minimality of $\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(\sigma^{-1}(a)/K)$, we obtain 
 $\sigma^{-1}(a)\in L\langle a,\ldots,\sigma^{N-1}(a)\rangle_{\Delta}^{\operatorname{alg}}$.
Iterating \(\sigma^{-1}\),  we see that \(L \langle a \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} = L \langle \{ \sigma^j(a) 
: j \in \mathbb{Z} \} \rangle_\Delta 
\subseteq L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta^{\operatorname{alg}}\). 
That is, \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/L) \leq N\).
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:minimal-sigma-degree}
If \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)\) is minimal, 
then \(\operatorname{SU}(a/K) \leq \operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/K)\).
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Taking into account the observation made just before this proof, 
it suffices to show that for natural numbers \(N\)
if \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/K) \leq N\), then 
\(\operatorname{SU}(a/K) \leq N\). We do this 
by induction on \(N\).  The case of \(N = 0\)
is trivial as \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/K) \leq 0\) implies by 
definition that \(a\) is model-theoretically algebraic over
\(K\) relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\) while the hypothesis on \(K\) 
that it is an inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-field
implies that this closure is the ordinary algebraic 
closure which would then imply that even relative to 
\(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\) the type \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)\) is 
algebraic, and, hence, not minimal.
For \(N+1\), if \(\operatorname{SU}(a/K) \geq N+2\),
then by definition of the Lascar rank we can find a 
small inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-extension 
\(L \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) of \(K\) so that 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} L\) and 
\(\operatorname{SU}(a/L) \geq N+1\).  By hypothesis, 
\(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/K) \leq N+1\).  
That is \(K \langle a \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} 
\subseteq K \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^N(a) \rangle_\Delta^{\operatorname{alg}}\). 
From the definition of \(\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}\), we would have 
\((\sigma^j(a))_{j\in \mathbb{Z}} \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).  
As the full sequence is algebraic over \((\sigma^j(a))_{j=0}^{N}\), 
we would have \(a, \ldots, \sigma^{N} a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\).  
Taking \(M \leq N\) minimal with \(a, \ldots, \sigma^M a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_K^\Delta L\), we would have by 
transitivity that \(\sigma^M a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}_{K \langle a, \ldots, 
\sigma^{M-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta} L \langle a, \ldots, 
\sigma^{M-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta \).  As 
\(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)\), and hence also \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(\sigma^M(a)/K)\), 
is minimal, this dependence yields that \(\sigma^M(a)\) is 
algebraic over \(L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{M-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta\).  Iterating \(\sigma\) and \(\sigma^{-1}\) as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:fork-minimal-finite}, this 
yields that \(L \langle a \rangle_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta} 
\subseteq L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{M-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta^{\operatorname{alg}} \subseteq  L \langle a, \ldots, \sigma^{N-1}(a) \rangle_\Delta^{\operatorname{alg}}\).  
That is, \(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/L) \leq N\).  Hence, 
by induction, \(\operatorname{SU}(a/L) \leq N\), 
contrary to our hypothesis.  With this we conclude.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- cor-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:at-most-oemga}
If \(\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)\) is minimal, then 
\(\operatorname{SU}(a/K) \leq \omega\).
<!-- end cor-4 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Suppose to the contrary that \(\operatorname{SU}(a/K) > \omega\).
Then by definition of the Lascar rank
we can find a small inversive \(\sigma\)-\(\Delta\)-extension 
\(L \subseteq \mathbb{U}\) of \(K\) so that 
\(a \mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\mathchardef \nn=12854\hss$\nn$\kern1.4\wd0\hss}\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss}\lower.9\ht0 \hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}_K L\) and \(\operatorname{SU}(a/L) 
\geq \omega\).  By Lemma~\ref{lem:fork-minimal-finite}, 
\(\operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/L) < \infty\).  But then 
Proposition~\ref{prop:minimal-sigma-degree} yields
\(\operatorname{SU}(a/L) \leq \operatorname{ord}_\sigma(a/L) < \omega\), 
contrary to our hypothesis.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:groups-as-infinite-rank}
Let \(G\) be a definable, connected, minimal group relative 
to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\).  Suppose that for some \(\ell \in \mathbb{Z}_+\)
we have a definable surjective homomorphism \(f:G \to G^{\sigma^\ell}\).  Then relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\) we have
\(\operatorname{SU}(G) \geq \omega\).
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Since a minimal group is commutative~\cite{Reineke}, 
we use additive notation.
Define the $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$-definable endomorphism
\[
\psi:G\longrightarrow G,
\qquad
\psi(x):=x-\sigma^{-\ell}(f(x)).
\]
For each $N\geq0$, let $H_N:=\ker(\psi^N)$, where
$\psi^0=\operatorname{id}_G$ and $H_0=\{0\}$.
Thus,
\(
H_1
=\{x\in G:\sigma^\ell(x)=f(x)\}.
\)



Let us now show how to use the geometric axioms to 
see that \(\psi\) is surjective and that 
\(H_1\) is infinite.

By \cite[Proposition~3.4(v)]{LS-DCFAm},
$(\mathbb U,\Delta,\sigma^\ell)$ is a model of $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$.
 Fix $b\in G(\mathbb U)$ and an arbitrary finite set
$E\subseteq G(\mathbb U)$.
Choose a small algebraically closed
$\sigma^\ell$-$\Delta$-subfield $K\subseteq\mathbb U$
containing $b$, the coordinates of $E$,
and the parameters defining $G$ and $f$.
Since $G$ is connected, it has a unique generic
differential type $p$ over $K$.
By saturation, choose $c\in G(\mathbb U)$ realizing $p$,
and set $d:=f(c)+\sigma^\ell(b)$.
Then $d$ realizes the unique generic differential
type $p^{\sigma^\ell}$ of $G^{\sigma^\ell}$ over $K$.

Let $Z$ be the Kolchin locus of $c$ over $K$, and let
$W$ be the Kolchin locus of $(c,d)$ over $K$.
Since $d$ realizes $p^{\sigma^\ell}$, its Kolchin locus is
$Z^{\sigma^\ell}$. Hence
\(
W\subseteq Z\times Z^{\sigma^\ell},
\)
and both coordinate projections are $\Delta$-dominant.
Consider the $K$-definable set
\[
D:=\{(x,y)\in G\times G^{\sigma^\ell}:
      x\notin E,\ y=f(x)+\sigma^\ell(b)\}.
\]
Note that $(c,d)\in D$, since $c$ is generic
over $K$ and $E\subseteq G(K)$ is finite.
By quantifier elimination in $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$, $D$ is
Kolchin-constructible. Because it contains the generic
point $(c,d)$ of $W$, there is a nonempty
$\Delta$-open subset $O\subseteq W$ contained in $D$.

Applying \cite[Fact~2.1]{LS-DCFAm} to
$W\subseteq Z\times Z^{\sigma^\ell}$ and its open subset $O$,
we obtain $x\in Z(\mathbb U)$ such that
\(
(x,\sigma^\ell(x))\in O\subseteq D.
\)
Therefore $x\in G(\mathbb U)\setminus E$ and
\(
\sigma^\ell(x)=f(x)+\sigma^\ell(b).
\)
Applying $\sigma^{-\ell}$ gives
\(
\psi(x)=x-\sigma^{-\ell}(f(x))=b.
\)

Taking $E=\varnothing$ and allowing arbitrary $b$
proves that $\psi$ is surjective.
Taking $b=0$ and allowing arbitrary finite $E$
shows that $H_1=\ker\psi$ is infinite. 
 

For every $N\geq0$, the restriction
$\psi^N:H_{N+1}\longrightarrow H_1$
is a surjective homomorphism with kernel $H_N$.
Indeed, if $x\in H_{N+1}$, then
$\psi(\psi^N(x))=0$, so $\psi^N(x)\in H_1$.
Conversely, given $z\in H_1$, surjectivity of $\psi^N$
provides $x\in G$ with $\psi^N(x)=z$, and then
$\psi^{N+1}(x)=\psi(z)=0$.
The kernel of this restriction is precisely $H_N$.
Therefore,
\(
H_{N+1}/H_N\cong H_1.
\)
In particular, $[H_{N+1}:H_N]$ is infinite.

Thus $G$ contains an increasing chain of
$\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$-definable subgroups
\(
\{0\}=H_0<H_1<H_2<\cdots,
\)
each of infinite index in the next. 
By the Lascar inequalities
\cite[Theorem~5.1.6(1)]{Wagner-simple},
\(
\operatorname{SU}(H_{N+1})
\geq
\operatorname{SU}(H_N)
+\operatorname{SU}(H_{N+1}/H_N)
\geq
\operatorname{SU}(H_N)+1.\)
It follows that $\operatorname{SU}(G)\geq\omega$.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- cor-5: body_md -->
\label{cor:rank-omega-type}
Let $K$ be a small $\sigma$-$\Delta$-field.
Suppose that $p=\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(a/K)$
is minimal and is the generic type of a connected
$K$-definable group $G$ relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$.
Suppose further that there is a $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$-definable
surjective homomorphism
\(
f:G\longrightarrow G^{\sigma^\ell}
\)
for some $\ell\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$.
Then $\operatorname{SU}(p)=\omega$ relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$.
<!-- end cor-5 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Since $G$ is connected and the 
$\Delta$-type $p$ is minimal, $G$ is strongly minimal relative
to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}$. Thus $p$ is the unique nonalgebraic
$\Delta$-type over $K$  on $G$.

Let $q$ be a generic type of $G$ over $K$ relative
to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$. Its $\Delta$-restriction is nonalgebraic:
otherwise, $q$ would contain a $\Delta$-formula
over $K$ with finitely many solutions and would
itself be algebraic. Consequently, $q$ extends $p$.

Generic types of a definable group in a supersimple
theory have the same $\operatorname{SU}$-rank as
the group. Hence Proposition~\ref{prop:groups-as-infinite-rank}
gives
\[
\operatorname{SU}(p)
\geq \operatorname{SU}(q)
=\operatorname{SU}(G)
\geq\omega.
\]
On the other hand, let $q$ be any complete
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-type over $K$ extending $p$,
and  $b\models q$. Since
$\operatorname{tp}_\Delta(b/K)=p$ is minimal,
Corollary~\ref{cor:at-most-oemga} gives
$\operatorname{SU}(q)=\operatorname{SU}(b/K)\leq\omega$.
Taking the supremum over all such completions $q$,
we obtain $\operatorname{SU}(p)\leq\omega$.
Therefore $\operatorname{SU}(p)=\omega$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- remark-17: body_md -->
The equality $\operatorname{SU}(p)=\omega$ concerns
$p$ as a partial $\sigma$-$\Delta$-type.
It does not assert that every complete
$\sigma$-$\Delta$-type extending $p$ has rank $\omega$.
In particular, it does not necessarily imply
$\operatorname{SU}(\operatorname{tp}_{\sigma\text{-}\Delta}(a/K))=\omega$
for the particular realization $a$ used to specify $p$.
However, the proof does show that some completion of $p$
has rank $\omega$.
<!-- end remark-17 -->
<!-- cor-6: body_md -->
\label{cor:generic-constant-as-sigma}
Let \(p \in S_\Delta(K)\) be the generic type of the 
field of constants.  That is \(p\) is axiomatized by 
\(\delta x = 0\) for \(\delta \in \Delta\) and 
\(g(x)\neq 0\) for every nonzero $g\in K[x]$.  Then \(p\) is
minimal relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\)  and \(\operatorname{SU}(p) = \omega\) 
relative to \(\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}\).
<!-- end cor-6 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Apply Corollary~\ref{cor:rank-omega-type} to the 
group \(G = (\{ x \in \mathbb{U} : \delta x = 0 
\text{ for all } \delta \in \Delta \}, +)\) and the definable homomorphism 
\(f:G \to G\) being the identity map given by \(x \mapsto x\).
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:Manin-kernel-isogenous}
Let $K$ be an algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\delta$-field.
Suppose that $A$ is a simple
abelian variety over $K$ that does not descend to
the constants; that is, $A$ is not isomorphic over
$\mathbb U$ to an abelian variety defined over
$\mathsf C$.
Let $p$ be the generic differential type of
the Manin kernel $A^\sharp$ over $K$.

Then  relative to $\operatorname{DCF}_{0,m}\operatorname{A}$, either $p$ remains minimal
or $\operatorname{SU}(p)=\omega$.
The first case occurs if and only if there is no
positive integer $\ell$ and isogeny
\(f:A\longrightarrow A^{\sigma^\ell}\).
 In that case, $p$ has a unique complete quantifier-free
$\sigma$-$\delta$-extension over $K$, this extension
is minimal, and every full completion of $p$ has
$\operatorname{SU}$-rank one.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
By \cite[Fact~5.1]{Casale-Freitag-Nagloo-I},
$A^\sharp$ is strongly minimal.
In particular, $p$ is minimal and stationary.
For each $\ell>0$, the generic $\Delta$-type of
$(A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp=
(A^\sharp)^{\sigma^\ell}$
is $p^{\sigma^\ell}$.
By \cite[Fact~5.6]{Casale-Freitag-Nagloo-I},
for every $\ell>0$,
\[
p\not\perp p^{\sigma^\ell}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
A\text{ and }A^{\sigma^\ell}\text{ are isogenous}.
\]

Suppose first that no such isogeny exists. 
Then $p\perp p^{\sigma^\ell}$ for every $\ell>0$.
By Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-sigma-hypertranscendence}, $p$ has a unique complete quantifier-free
$\sigma$-$\delta$-extension, and this extension
is minimal.  It remains to show that every full completion of $p$
has $\operatorname{SU}$-rank one.
Let $q$ be such a completion, let $a\models q$, and
let $E\supseteq K$ be a small parameter set such that
$\operatorname{tp}_{\sigma\text{-}\delta}(a/E)$
is nonalgebraic. Set
\(
L:=\operatorname{acl}^{\sigma\text{-}\delta}(E).
\)
By \cite[Proposition~3.1(i)]{LS-DCFAm}, $L$ is an
algebraically closed $\sigma$-$\delta$-field.
Moreover, $a$ is nonalgebraic over $L$.
The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-sigma-hypertranscendence}
therefore gives
\(
a\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\delta}_K L,
\)
and hence $a\mathop{\mathpalette\setbox0=\hbox{$x$}\kern\wd0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\mid$\hss} \lower.9\ht0\hbox to 0pt{\hss$\smile$\hss}\kern\wd0{}}^{\sigma\text{-}\delta}_K E$.
Thus every nonalgebraic extension of $q$ is
nonforking, so every forking extension of $q$ is
algebraic. Since $q$ itself is nonalgebraic,
$\operatorname{SU}(q)=1$.

 
Suppose now that there is an isogeny
$f:A\to A^{\sigma^\ell}$ for some $\ell>0$, which necessarily will be defined over \(K\)~\cite{Chow-AV}. 
We show that $f$ restricts to a definable surjective
homomorphism $A^\sharp\to (A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp$.



Since   $f$ sends $\operatorname{Tor}(A)$, the set of torsion points of $A$, to $\operatorname{Tor}(A^{\sigma^\ell})$,  
$\operatorname{Tor}(A)\subseteq f^{-1}((A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp)$.
As the Manin kernel $A^\sharp$ is the  smallest $\Delta$-definable subgroup containing $\operatorname{Tor}(A)$\cite[Lemma~3.3(i)]{BBP-semiabelian}, \(A^\sharp\subseteq f^{-1}((A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp)\),  and thus
\(f(A^\sharp)\subseteq (A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp.
\)
 Since $f$ has finite kernel and $A^\sharp$ is
infinite, $f(A^\sharp)$ is an infinite $\Delta$-definable subgroup of $(A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp$.
Strong minimality of $(A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp$ therefore gives $f(A^\sharp)=(A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp$.

We have therefore obtained a $K$-definable
surjective homomorphism
\[
f|_{A^\sharp}:
A^\sharp\longrightarrow (A^{\sigma^\ell})^\sharp=
 (A^\sharp)^{\sigma^\ell}.
\]
The strongly minimal group $A^\sharp$ is connected:
a proper definable subgroup of finite index would
be infinite and coinfinite.
Corollary~\ref{cor:rank-omega-type} now gives
$\operatorname{SU}(p)=\omega$.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
